<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Assuming the stated critical-height, local-renormalization, and spin-field inputs from the companion papers, we determine the center magnetization of the planar XY model in a square with aligned boundary spins at its mass-defined critical threshold. As $`n\to\infty`$, the magnetization is $`A_{\mathrm{XY}}n^{-1/8}(\log n)^{1/16}(1+o(1))`$, where $`A_{\mathrm{XY}}`$ is a finite, strictly positive model-specific constant.

- **claim-center-magnetization-asymptotic** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Using the height, renormalization, and spin-field inputs from (OpenAI 2026a, 2026b, 2026c) [[B](#ref-B), [X](#ref-X), [S](#ref-S)] stated in Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a>, there is a constant $A_{\mathrm{XY}}\in(0,\infty)$ such that $$\label{eq:main} a_n=A_{\mathrm{XY}}\,n^{-1/8}(\log n)^{1/16}(1+o(1)) \qquad(n\to\infty).$$
  - Explanation: Conditional on the companion height, local renormalization, and spin-field inputs, the aligned-boundary center magnetization has a precise algebraic decay with a logarithmic multiplier and a finite positive model-specific amplitude.
- **claim-fixed-boundary-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-duality)): Work at $b=b_c$. Let $G_M$ be the free graph of the $M^2$ unit square faces in $[-n,n]^2$, where $M=2n$. If $l$ has circulation $2\pi$ around the central primal vertex and zero circulation around all other interior primal vertices, then $$a_n=F_{G_{2n}}^l>0.$$ The graph retains the edges crossing primal bonds incident to the fixed spin boundary.
  - Explanation: Fourier duality identifies the center observable with a positive height partition ratio carrying a central circulation, while retaining the dual bonds required by the fixed spin boundary.
- **claim-finite-graph-comparisons** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-comparisons)): The comparisons in (OpenAI 2026b, Lemmas 2.2 and 2.3) [[X](#ref-X)] hold on every fixed primary graph, also with independent Gaussian observation noises.* 1. *Adding centered precision decreases centered covariance and centered real moment generating functions. Covariance in an affine sector, including after a real tilt, dominates centered covariance. In particular, for a centered inversion-symmetric law, $\log\mathbb Ee^X\ge\frac12\operatorname{Var}(X)$.* 2. *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same positive precision is added in the two sectors. The shifts may be integral connections, prescribed equality values, or observation centers. Monotonicity also holds with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent; that tilted ratio need not be at most one. Infinite penalties may impose nonempty affine constraints.* 3. *Invariant centered characteristic coefficients are nonnegative and increase with precision. In the finite-graph situations used here they are positive, and the Hessian of their logarithm at any phase is at least its Hessian at zero. A phase on a free component is invariant whenever its coefficient total is an integer.* 4. *With any extra centered precision, for each fixed integer $r\ge1$ and real $t$, $$\label{inp:reference-cost} \operatorname{Var}(X)\le C_b\mathcal R(w),\qquad \mathbb E\|X\|^r\le C_{r,b}\mathcal R(w)^{r/2},\qquad \mathbb Ee^{tX/\sqrt{\mathcal R(w)}}\le C_{t,b}.$$ The last assertion is used for $\mathcal R(w)>0$; a zero-cost statistic vanishes. One may minimize the cost over flows with the prescribed divergence and grounded vertices.
  - Explanation: The imported finite-graph comparisons control centered precision, affine sectors, invariant characteristics, and moments of edge statistics with independent observation noise. The tilted ratio and the un-tilted ratio have different upper-bound guarantees.
- **claim-critical-height-and-pin-limits** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-height)): For the mass threshold $b_c$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $0<b_c<\infty$, $m(b_c)=0$, and the primary height coefficient of (OpenAI 2026b) [[X](#ref-X)] satisfies $$\label{inp:critical-coefficient} a(b_c)=8\pi.$$ At a general positive coefficient $a=a(b)>0$, the following statements hold.* 1. *On free rectangles tending after scaling to a fixed nondegenerate rectangle $U$, the tests $\langle h,A_{U_N}f(\cdot/N)\rangle$, for smooth $f$ near $\overline U$, have joint Gaussian Laplace and moment limits with covariance $a\int_U\nabla f\cdot\nabla g$, where the lattice mesh is $N^{-1}$. Mean-free density smears have covariance $a(-\Delta_{U,N})^{-1}$; bounded piecewise continuous densities and face smears, centered by a volume smear, are allowed.* 2. *In a fixed polygonal geometry made of finitely many rectangular pieces, with positive-width passages and box pin regions $p$, the corresponding limits have precision $$a^{-1}\int_U\|\nabla v\|^2, \qquad v\in H^1(U),\quad v=0\ \hbox{on }p.$$ Free components are taken modulo constants. A fixed finite-rank nonnegative quadratic penalty in box averages gives the corresponding Gaussian penalty; if it controls all free constants, those constants are integrated in the limit. Bounded-step coherent rounding is permitted.* 3. *The fractional free mean modulo $2\pi$ tends to uniform measure, independently of the Gaussian observations. If $u_m$ is the uniform probability on an $m$-square and $t_m$ is a zero-total profile of bounded reference cost, then for every fixed nonzero integer $k$, $$\mathbb Ee^{i\langle h,ku_m+t_m\rangle}\longrightarrow0.$$ Writing $\bar\rho_m$ for the average of the four face probabilities and $z_m=\mathbb Ee^{i\langle h,\bar\rho_m\rangle}$, one has $$\label{inp:face-ratio} \liminf_{m\to\infty}\frac{z_{Gm}}{z_m} \ge c_bG^{2-a/(4\pi)}$$ for sufficiently large fixed integers $G$, with $c_b>0$ independent of $G$.
  - Explanation: The height input identifies the critical Gaussian coefficient and supplies rectangle, mixed pinned-geometry, and fractional-mean limits. These limits provide the continuum comparisons used in entry and bulk diagnostics.
- **claim-center-lower-bound-and-ratio** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-regular-variation)): There is $c>0$ such that $a_n\ge cn^{-1/2}$ for all $n\ge1$. If $n_k,n'_k\to\infty$ are integer sequences and $n'_k/n_k\to d\in(0,\infty)$, then $$\label{inp:center-ratio} \frac{a_{n'_k}}{a_{n_k}}\longrightarrow d^{-1/8}.$$
  - Explanation: A rough positive lower bound makes absolute magnetic errors relatively negligible. Sequential dilation ratios later transfer the geometric-size normalization to arbitrary integer sizes without determining the slowly varying factor by themselves.
- **claim-observation-regulator** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-regulator)): The regulator in <a href="#inp:variational-regulator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:variational-regulator">[inp:variational-regulator]</a> is finite, nonnegative, homogeneous quadratic, and constant invariant. It is monotone under support enlargement and additive on compatible separated supports, and $$0\le V_j(X,\psi)\le CE_D(B_s\alpha\psi) \le C\langle\psi,A_D\psi\rangle.$$ The shell and terminal regulator estimates of (OpenAI 2026a, Lemmas 4.4 and 9.2) [[B](#ref-B)] hold with $W_u^\kappa$ replaced by $W_u^\kappa e^{V_j}$, at a cost $C^{\|X\|}$. For a small connected input the output retains the half-exponent reserve $W_{Lu}^{\kappa/2}$; the coefficient of the observation regulator is unchanged. The localizations and analytic partition map retain their contraction and nonlinear estimates in <a href="#inp:norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:norm">[inp:norm]</a>.
  - Explanation: The homogeneous observation regulator remains controlled by local gradient energy and preserves the integration reserves and map estimates needed for analytic activities.
- **claim-exact-local-partition-map** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-partition-map)): In the norms above, shell integration and designated local subtractions give an exact identity $$\label{inp:exact-map} \mathbb E\mathcal Z_u(K;\psi+\zeta) =\prod_{b\in\mathcal B_{Lu}}(1+a_b) \mathcal Z_{Lu}(K';\psi), \qquad \operatorname{Cov}(\zeta)=\Gamma_u.$$ The map is complex analytic in small activity inputs, uniformly in finite volume, and its nonlinear remainder is bounded by $C_{L,\mathfrak A}\varepsilon^2$ when the activity norm and separately measured singleton coefficients are at most $\varepsilon$. It preserves the common-shift period and all symmetries respected by the localizations. The linear map restricted to large supports has arbitrarily small operator norm, relative to the input activity norm, after choosing $\mathfrak A$ sufficiently large at fixed $L$.* *For small field-even neutral bulk inputs, subtraction of the constant and quadratic gradient term gives $CL^{-3}$ before counting fine placements. Subtracting only the constant gives $CL^{-2}$ for even inputs and $CL^{-1}$ without evenness. There are respectively $O(L^2)$, $O(L)$, and $O(1)$ placements at a bulk block, a specified wall, and a fixed mark. At $\alpha^2=8\pi$, the fundamental charge has gain $O(L^{-2})$; for field-even bulk inputs, subtraction of its constant-background cosine coefficient gives an additional $L^{-1}$. Higher charges have summable stronger gains.
  - Explanation: Shell integration has an exact partition identity with designated scalar extraction. Analytic nonlinear bounds and localization gains distinguish bulk, wall, and marked placements.
- **claim-balanced-parity-flow** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inp-balanced-map)): For the critical reference and small, even, translation- and square-covariant plane activities, the preceding coordinates satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \label{inp:parity-equations} u_+-u={}&-H_jZ^2+ O\bigl((\|u\|+Z^2)(Z^2+\lVert B_e\rVert)+\lVert B_e\rVert^2 +\lVert B_o\rVert(\|Z\|+\lVert B_o\rVert)\bigr),\nonumber\\ Z_+-Z={}&-b_juZ+ O\bigl((u^2+Z^2+\lVert B_e\rVert)\|Z\| +( \|u\|+Z^2+\lVert B_e\rVert+\lVert B_o\rVert^2)\lVert B_o\rVert\bigr),\nonumber\\ \lVert B_{e,+}\rVert\le{}&\theta_1\lVert B_e\rVert +C(\|Z\|+\lVert B_o\rVert)^2,\nonumber\\ \lVert B_{o,+}\rVert\le{}&\theta_1\lVert B_o\rVert +C(\|u\|+Z^2+\lVert B_e\rVert)\|Z\|, \qquad \theta_1<1. \end{aligned}$$ The estimates include their differentiated analytic Taylor bounds. The coefficients are bounded above and away from zero, and $$\label{inp:coefficient-limits} H_j\to H>0,\qquad b_j\to2\log L.$$ These are the map in Lemma 10.6 and the coefficient calculation in Proposition 8.5 of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)].
  - Explanation: Balanced coordinates separate the marginal gradient and fundamental charge from contracting parity remainders. The imported coefficient limits do not themselves establish residence in the small tube or the quantitative absolute-index rate.
- **claim-finite-volume-cutoff-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-prep-cutoff)): On a compatible torus or free square of side $M$, the absolute change in the partition sum caused by the product of <a href="#prep:cutoff-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prep:cutoff-definition">[prep:cutoff-definition]</a>, divided by the centered partition sum without cutoffs, is at most $$\label{eq:cutoff-error} C(M/s)^2 e^{-c_1s}.$$ The assertion includes a free square with the integral connection $l$. On a torus it also holds, with a smaller $c_1>0$, after inserting a bounded test, a fixed power of a smooth mean-free smear, or a fixed real or complex exponential test of such smears.
  - Explanation: The entire face cutoff changes centered-normalized partition sums by an exponentially small observation-scale error, including the pierced square and the specified torus tests.
- **claim-local-lifts-and-square-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prep-lifts)): Let $r$ be the square minimizer at side $M$ and $r_\infty$ the plane height just defined. If an axis rectangle and its fixed collar avoid the source, choose an integral gauge potential $g$ there with $\partial g=l$. The local real lift is $\lambda=(r+g)/\alpha$, and $\partial\lambda=\eta_l/\alpha$. At interior supports meeting the source, the translation from the pierced-plane calculation to the square is $$\label{eq:lift-difference} \gamma_M=(r-r_\infty)/\alpha.$$ In lattice units, for the fixed derivative orders used in the maps, $$\|\nabla^a\eta_l(x)\|\le C_a(1+\operatorname{dist}(x,\text{source}))^{-1-a}.$$ In an inner fixed fraction of the square, $$\label{eq:lift-derivatives} \|\nabla^a\gamma_M\|\le C_aM^{-a}, \qquad 1\le a\le5.$$ The connection estimate includes the reflected stencils at free walls. The affine contribution $(B_sr)_{c'}-(B_sr)_c+l_f$ to every retained observation difference is bounded uniformly in $s$ and $M$.
  - Explanation: Away from the source, an integral gauge yields a local real lift. Near the source, finite-square activities use a smooth square-to-plane translation whose positive derivatives are small in the square side.
- **claim-large-observation-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-prep-large-field)): For every preparation graph $G$ and every real observation vector $y$, $$\label{eq:large-observation} \frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)} \le\exp\left\{-c\sum_{\substack{f\in\mathcal F(G)\\\|d_f\|\le2T}} d_f^2\right\}.$$ Consequently, with a possibly smaller constant $c>0$ independent of $R,P,s$, $$\label{prep:face-saving} \frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)} \prod_{f\in\mathcal F(G)}\chi_T(d_f) \le e^{-c\sum_{f\in\mathcal F(G)}d_f^2}.$$
  - Explanation: Tilted comparisons give quadratic suppression on controlled face differences, and the cutoff supplies suppression on the remaining faces. Their combination gives a strict large-observation saving.
- **claim-affine-shape-localization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-localization)): On each open preparation graph there are a quadratic form $L_G$ with a fixed-radius stencil in the faces, and a symmetric matrix $N_G$ on those faces, such that $$\label{prep:shape-decomposition} E_G^l(y)=L_G(d)+\sum_{f,g\in\mathcal F(G)}N_G(f,g)d_fd_g,$$ where $$\label{prep:trial-energy} 0\le L_G(d)\le C\sum_fd_f^2 +C\log(2s)\sum_z\kappa_z(d)^2.$$ The forms and matrices may be defined on arbitrary face arrays $d$, without imposing a curl constraint. Their nonlocal entries satisfy $$\label{prep:matrix-decay} \|N_G(f,g)\|\le Ce^{-c\operatorname{dist}(f,g)}.$$ Their dependence on the graph is exponentially localized as well. In particular, if faces in a set $D$ are deleted, extend both matrices by zero on missing faces. With a fixed stencil enlargement understood in the distances, $$\label{prep:matrix-cut} \|(N_G-N_{G\setminus D})(f,g)\| \le C\exp\left\{-c\left(\operatorname{dist}(f,g) +\min\{\operatorname{dist}(f,D),\operatorname{dist}(g,D)\}\right) \right\}.$$ The local stencils agree wherever the graphs agree. For the actual one-source data this implies $$\label{eq:affine-shape-bound} E_G^l(y)\le C\sum_{f\in\mathcal F(G)}d_f^2 +C\log(2s)\mathbf 1_{\{\text{pierced star present}\}}.$$
  - Explanation: The affine shape energy separates into a local stencil and an exponentially localized correction matrix. The local part contains the circulation cost, while graph-deletion effects remain exponentially localized.
- **claim-mesoscopic-analytic-entry** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-entry)): Choose the regulator reserves as in <a href="#eq:entry-admissible-reserve" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:entry-admissible-reserve">[eq:entry-admissible-reserve]</a>, and fix compatible parameters for the local maps of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)], including the block factor $L$, the support geometry, the activity weight $\mathfrak A$, and the analytic radius. A fixed finite collection of stronger weights and entry-admissible regulator regimes may be specified. For every $\epsilon>0$, one can choose a fixed dyadic $q$, an arbitrarily large dyadic $R$, and then $s_0$, such that the following conclusions hold for every compatible dyadic $s\ge s_0$, with $P=R^5$ and $m=qRs$.* 1. *In the ordinary case, averaging the observation noise and the reference covariance below $m$ gives local activities $K_m$ satisfying $$\label{eq:entry-small} \lVert K_m\rVert_{m,\mathfrak A,V}\le\epsilon.$$ This holds on finite plane supports, on compatible tori, and on free squares whose sides are divisible by $sP$ and are at least $sP$. Up to a positive field-independent scalar, the corresponding subset partition function is exactly the averaged cutoff interaction. Activities with their required clearance from a wall or a torus identification are the corresponding plane activities. The construction is real, even, and covariant under the block translations and square symmetries that preserve the chosen hierarchy.* 2. *In a pierced free square in which the compulsory footprint and its required collars fit, the same scalar as in its ordinary counterpart may be extracted. The remaining partition function is $$\label{eq:entry-linear-partition} \mathcal Z_m^{(1)}(K_m^l,I_m;\psi),$$ with exactly one insertion activity $I_m$. Its supports contain the mark block and the compulsory cover just specified. The background activities $K_m^l$ are arbitrarily small in their prescribed norms, and $$\label{eq:entry-insertion} \lVert I_m\rVert_{m,\mathfrak A,V} \le C_R s^{C_R}.$$ Here and below $C_R$ may also depend on the fixed downstream norm and geometric parameters, but not on $s$ or on the square side. Ordinary occupied supports remain separated from the insertion by the original compatibility rule.* 3. *A support is called *shift-good* if its spanning rectangle, enlarged by $Hm$, misses the magnetic mark. The fixed constant $H$ is chosen from the local-map geometry. On such supports the pierced background is the ordinary background evaluated at the local lift $\psi+\lambda$, where $$\partial\lambda=\eta_l/\alpha.$$ On interior supports near the mark, including insertion supports, the activities are copies of the pierced-plane calculation evaluated at $\psi+\gamma_M$, with $\gamma_M$ and its derivative bounds as in <a href="#eq:lift-difference" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-difference">[eq:lift-difference]</a> and <a href="#eq:lift-derivatives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-derivatives">[eq:lift-derivatives]</a>. Copying always includes the collars and numerical dependence required by the activity.* *The assertions hold simultaneously in the fixed finite collection of reserve regimes. Their constants are uniform after the geometric parameters have been fixed.
  - Explanation: Ordered parameter choices produce arbitrarily small ordinary and pierced background activities in full analytic norms. The source component remains a compulsory linear insertion with a polynomial bound, and copying includes numerical dependencies and collars.
- **claim-uniform-absolute-index-maps** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bulk-parity)): The coordinate changes and parity-resolved maps of Proposition <a href="#inp:balanced-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:balanced-map">8</a> have a common analytic neighborhood and common bounds for all sufficiently large entry indices $i(J)$. At absolute index $k$, their two scalar quadratic coefficients obey $$\label{eq:bulk-coefficient-rate} H_k=H+O((1+k)^{-2}),\qquad b_k=b_*+O((1+k)^{-2}),\qquad H>0,\quad b_*=2\log L.$$ The coefficients $H_k,b_k$ are independent of the entry index. The constants in the parity remainder bounds are uniform in the $J$-history.
  - Explanation: Coordinate changes have a common analytic neighborhood as entry varies. The quadratic coefficients depend on absolute scale rather than entry history and approach their limits with a quantitative error sufficient for the trajectory argument.
- **claim-periodic-physical-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bulk-physical-diagnostics)): As $N\to\infty$ with $s/N\to0$, locally uniformly for complex $w$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb Ee^{wX_N}&\longrightarrow e^{agw^2/2}, \label{eq:bulk-physical-gradient}\\ \mathbb E(\chi_Ne^{wX_N})-\mathbb E\chi_N\,e^{agw^2/2} &\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:bulk-physical-phase} \end{aligned}$$ All fixed source derivatives converge as well. For $s/N\le P^{-1}$, the discrepancies can be made uniformly small by first increasing $R$, hence $P$, and then increasing $s$. The same statements hold with the cutoff on every stopping torus of a $J$-history, uniformly up to index $8J$.
  - Explanation: The periodic long-wave transform approaches its Gaussian value, and the mean phase factorizes against that transform. Source derivatives and cutoff versions have the stated uniformity and order of parameter choices.
- **claim-no-exit-and-window-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bulk-window-vanishing)): For every sufficiently small prescribed tube radius, the entry parameters can be chosen so that all $J$-histories, for sufficiently large $J$, remain in that tube through index $8J$. The stopped torus remainders remain in a fixed comparable tube. Moreover, for every fixed $0<c<8$, $$\label{eq:bulk-window-vanishing} \sup_{cJ\le k\le8J} \bigl(\|u_k^{(J)}\|+\|Z_k^{(J)}\| +\lVert B_{e,k}^{(J)}\rVert+\lVert B_{o,k}^{(J)}\rVert\bigr) \longrightarrow0.$$ The analogous stopped torus remainder quantities tend to zero.
  - Explanation: Physical diagnostics prevent a first exit from the small tube. Contraction and vanishing diagnostic discrepancies then force all coordinates and stopped torus remainders to vanish on interior windows.
- **claim-positive-odd-coordinate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bulk-odd)): On every fixed window $cJ\le k\le8J$, $0<c<8$, and for large $J$, the period convention may be kept unchanged and $$\label{eq:bulk-odd-estimates} Z_k^{(J)}>0,\qquad -\log Z_k^{(J)}=o(J),\qquad \lVert B_{e,k}^{(J)}\rVert\le C(Z_k^{(J)})^2, \qquad \lVert B_{o,k}^{(J)}\rVert\le C\bigl(\|u_k^{(J)}\|+(Z_k^{(J)})^2\bigr)Z_k^{(J)}.$$ The $o(J)$ and the constants are uniform on the window.
  - Explanation: The original periodic mean phase rules out exponentially decaying odd trajectories. The unchanged period convention consequently has a positive fundamental coordinate, with even and odd remainders controlled by their parity forcing.
- **claim-common-physical-readouts** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bulk-readouts)): For every $0<c<8$, uniformly on $cJ\le k\le8J$, $$\begin{aligned} U_k&=\frac{a}{1-u_k^{(J)}}+O((Z_k^{(J)})^2)+O(e^{-c'J}), \label{eq:bulk-U-readout}\\ Y_k&=M_kZ_k^{(J)} +O\bigl((\|u_k^{(J)}\|+(Z_k^{(J)})^2)Z_k^{(J)}\bigr) +O(e^{-c'J}). \label{eq:bulk-Y-readout} \end{aligned}$$ Here $c'>0$ may depend on $c$, and $M_k$ is bounded above and below by positive constants and is common to all histories, up to an exponentially small error which is included in the display. The coordinate changes also give $$\label{eq:bulk-coordinate-conversion} t_k=u_k+O(u_k^2+Z_k^2),\qquad z_k=\ell_k^{-1}Z_k+O((\|u_k\|+Z_k^2)Z_k),\qquad \lVert\mathcal R_k\rVert\le C(u_k^2+Z_k^2),$$ where $\ell_k$ is the absolute-index fundamental balancing factor and is bounded above and below positively.
  - Explanation: The same microscopic variance and mean-phase observables read the marginal coordinates of every history at a common absolute index. The phase multiplier is common and positive, while undoing the coordinate changes gives quadratic remainder control.
- **claim-sparse-splice-asymptotics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trajectory-sparse-splice)): Let $H,b>0$, let $j_0\ge1$, and let $\mathcal Q$ be a set of integers larger than $j_0$. Suppose that the increasing enumeration $(q_r)$ of $\mathcal Q$ satisfies $$q_{r+1}\ge \rho q_r \qquad\text{for some fixed }\rho>1.$$ The set $\mathcal Q$ may also be finite or empty. For every integer $j\ge j_0$, let $(u_j,Z_j)$ and $(\widetilde u_{j+1},\widetilde Z_{j+1})$ be real states with positive second coordinates. Assume that both coordinates of every state tend to zero as its scale index tends to infinity. Suppose that the ordinary evolution satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \widetilde u_{j+1}-u_j &=-H_j Z_j^2 +O\bigl((\|u_j\|+Z_j^2)Z_j^2\bigr), \label{eq:trajectory-ordinary-u}\\ \frac{\widetilde Z_{j+1}}{Z_j} &=1-b_j u_j+O(u_j^2+Z_j^2), \label{eq:trajectory-ordinary-z}\\ H_j&=H+O(j^{-2}),\qquad b_j=b+O(j^{-2}). \label{eq:trajectory-coefficients} \end{aligned}$$ For $q>j_0$ with $q\notin\mathcal Q$, assume $(u_q,Z_q)=(\widetilde u_q,\widetilde Z_q)$. At every $q\in\mathcal Q$, assume instead $$\begin{aligned} \|u_q-\widetilde u_q\|&\le C\widetilde Z_q^2, \label{eq:trajectory-jump-u}\\ \|Z_q-\widetilde Z_q\| &\le C\bigl(\|\widetilde u_q\|+\widetilde Z_q^2\bigr) \widetilde Z_q. \label{eq:trajectory-jump-z} \end{aligned}$$ All constants in these hypotheses are uniform in the scale index. Then, with $x_*=(H/b)^{1/2}$, $$\begin{aligned} \frac{u_j}{Z_j}&=x_*+O(j^{-1}), \label{eq:trajectory-ratio}\\ \frac1{Z_j}&=b x_*j+O(\log j), \label{eq:trajectory-inverse}\\ u_j&=\frac1{bj}+O\left(\frac{\log j}{j^2}\right),\qquad Z_j=\frac1{\sqrt{bH}\,j} +O\left(\frac{\log j}{j^2}\right). \label{eq:trajectory-asymptotics} \end{aligned}$$ The same estimates hold for the states immediately before jumps.
  - Explanation: Positive states tending to zero under the marginal recurrences retain the critical asymptotics when history changes obey the stated bounds and are geometrically separated. A change at a fixed index is distinguished from ordinary evolution time.
- **claim-overlap-coordinate-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trajectory-overlap)): Consider two bulk histories with comparable window parameters $J,J'$. At an index $j$ belonging to fixed positive-fraction windows of both histories, write $(u,Z)$ and $(u',Z')$ for their balanced marginal coordinates. For all sufficiently large indices, $$\|u'-u\|\le CZ^2, \qquad \|Z'-Z\|\le C(\|u\|+Z^2)Z. \label{eq:trajectory-overlap}$$ The constants are uniform when the comparison factors for the two windows are fixed.
  - Explanation: Common physical readouts compare marginal coordinates of histories with comparable windows. Positivity and subexponential lower bounds absorb the exponential errors before the comparison is divided by the phase multiplier.
- **claim-bulk-overlap-window-asymptotics** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bulk-window)): Fix the block factor $L$ and the other parameters chosen for the bulk maps. For every sufficiently large dyadic $J$, uniformly in the $J$-history at integer indices $J/2\le j\le3J$, its unbalanced gradient coordinate $t_j$, fundamental coordinate $z_j$, and plane remainder $\mathcal R_j$ satisfy $$t_j=\frac1{2j\log L} +O\left(\frac{\log j}{j^2}\right), \qquad \|z_j\|=O(j^{-1}), \qquad \\|\mathcal R_j\\|=O(j^{-2}). \label{eq:trajectory-bulk-window}$$ The remainder norm is the unbalanced plane activity norm.
  - Explanation: Sparse splicing determines the leading gradient coefficient on a selected path. Overlap comparisons transfer that asymptotic and the charge and remainder bounds to each stated history window.
- **claim-common-magnetic-normalization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-magnetic)): For all sufficiently large $j$, the exact maps from entry scale $i$ to stop $h$ can be chosen with the following properties.* 1. *At the stop, in the fixed norm regimes for the ordinary and defect activities, $$\label{eq:mag-small-backgrounds} \max_{Q\in\{M,LM\}} \bigl(\lVert K^0_{h,Q}\rVert+\lVert K^l_{h,Q}\rVert\bigr)\le \frac{C}{j}.$$ The bulk copies satisfy the sharper conclusion of Proposition <a href="#prop:bulk-window" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:bulk-window">21</a>. On shift-good supports, pierced activities are exactly ordinary activities evaluated at the local lift. Interior pierced activities copy the plane calculation at the translate $\gamma_Q$, whenever their required clearance holds.* 2. *Let $a^\nu_{k,Q,b}$ be the designated scalar coefficient at coarse block $b$ in step $k$, for $\nu=0,l$, and set $$\label{eq:mag-background-products} P^\nu_Q=\prod_{k=i}^{h-1}\prod_b(1+a^\nu_{k,Q,b}).$$ All factors are real and positive. The ratios agree exactly: $$\label{eq:mag-common-background-ratio} \frac{P^l_M}{P^0_M}=\frac{P^l_{LM}}{P^0_{LM}}=:\rho_j.$$ For every fixed $\epsilon_2>0$, the initial smallness choices can be made so that $\|\log\rho_j\|\le\epsilon_2j$ for all sufficiently large $j$.* 3. *There are a real scalar $\sigma_Q$ and residual insertion activities $I_{h,Q}$ such that integration of the entry insertion over all shells below $v$ gives the exact field identity $$\label{eq:mag-insertion-identity} \mathbb E_{i:h}\mathscr I_i(K^l_{i,Q},I_{i,Q};\phi) =P^l_Q\left\{ \sigma_Q\mathscr Z_h(K^l_{h,Q};\phi) +\mathscr I_h(K^l_{h,Q},I_{h,Q};\phi)\right\}.$$ Here $\mathbb E_{i:h}$ denotes successive shell integration, with the remaining field $\phi$ held fixed. The scalar is common to the two squares, $$\label{eq:mag-common-insertion-scalar} \sigma_M=\sigma_{LM}=: \sigma_j,$$ and every support of $I_{h,Q}$ contains the designated mark block. Moreover, $$\label{eq:mag-insertion-bound} \lVert I_{h,Q}\rVert\le C_Rs^{C_R}L^{-2(h-i)/3}, \qquad Q\in\{M,LM\}.$$* *The products in <a href="#eq:mag-background-products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mag-background-products">[eq:mag-background-products]</a> contain only the RG extraction factors. They do not include the common entry scalar or the harmonic-energy factor of the Gaussian sector law.
  - Explanation: Exact local maps in the concentric squares preserve copied backgrounds, positive extraction products, and a common insertion scalar. Keeping the insertion linear allows its residual to contract without requiring its initial norm to be small.
- **claim-terminal-partition-expansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-partition)): For the backgrounds of Proposition <a href="#prop:magnetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:magnetic">22</a>, $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal Z_Q^\varepsilon&=1+O_L(D^2/j), \label{eq:terminal-partition-small}\\ \log\mathcal Z_Q^\varepsilon &=\sum_X\mathbb E_{*,Q}K^\varepsilon_{h,Q}(X;\psi) +O_L(D^4/j^2). \label{eq:terminal-log} \end{aligned}$$ They are real and positive for all sufficiently large $j$. Moreover, $$\|\mathcal J_Q\| \leq C_LD^2\\|I_{h,Q}\\|. \label{eq:terminal-insertion}$$ These estimates hold with the fixed regulator and activity-weight reserves used in the magnetic construction.
  - Explanation: Reserved regulator estimates control terminal partition sums despite the growing number of blocks. The expansion separates the linear activity contribution from higher degrees and bounds the residual insertion.
- **claim-relative-insertion-removal** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-scalar)): There is $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $j$, $\sigma_j>0$ and $$\log a_{Q/2} =-\frac{E_Q}{2\alpha^2}+\log\rho_j+\log\sigma_j +\log\mathcal Z_Q^l-\log\mathcal Z_Q^0+O(e^{-cj}) \label{eq:terminal-log-ratio}$$ for both $Q=M$ and $Q=LM$.
  - Explanation: Residual insertion and cutoff errors are exponentially small relative to the crude physical lower bound. Positivity of the physical ratio then establishes positivity of the common extracted scalar and permits the logarithmic identity.
- **claim-first-order-terminal-double-difference** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-first-order)): With the running gradient coordinate $t_h$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:bulk-window" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:bulk-window">21</a>, $$\Delta_j =\frac{t_h}{2\alpha^2}(E_{LM}-E_M) +O_L\left(\frac{D^2}{j^2}+\frac1{Dj}\right). \label{eq:terminal-first-order}$$
  - Explanation: The leading terminal magnetic difference is the running gradient coordinate multiplied by the deterministic connection energy increment. Bulk remainder, even wall translation, and smooth near-mark covariance comparisons give the stated error.
- **claim-square-green-energy-increment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-green)): Let $G_n$ be the Green function of the unscaled Laplacian killed on the boundary vertices of the spin square $\Lambda_n$. For the connections in the dual squares of sides $M=2n$ and $LM$, there is $c_4>0$ such that $$E_{LM}-E_M =2\pi\log L+O_L(n^{-c_4}). \label{eq:terminal-energy}$$
  - Explanation: The killed unscaled Laplacian fixes the energy normalization. Finite-range cancellation and quantitative convergence of smoothed terminal diagonals give the logarithmic connection-energy increment with power accuracy.
- **claim-summable-enlargement-ratio** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ratio)): For $n_j=L^j/2$, there exists $\epsilon>0$ such that $$\log a_{n_{j+1}}-\log a_{n_j} =-\frac{\log L}{8}+\frac1{16j} +O_L(j^{-1-\epsilon}). \label{eq:terminal-ratio}$$
  - Explanation: Common magnetic scalars cancel exactly. The energy increment and running gradient coefficient leave a summably accurate logarithmic enlargement ratio, which determines the finite positive amplitude.

        "anchor": "stmt-inp-partition-map",
        "exact_quote": "In the norms above, shell integration and designated local subtractions give an exact identity $$\\label{inp:exact-map}\n \\mathbb E\\mathcal Z_u(K;\\psi+\\zeta)\n   =\\prod_{b\\in\\mathcal B_{Lu}}(1+a_b)\n                                      \\mathcal Z_{Lu}(K';\\psi),\n \\qquad \\operatorname{Cov}(\\zeta)=\\Gamma_u.$$ The map is complex analytic in small activity inputs, uniformly in finite volume, and its nonlinear remainder is bounded by $C_{L,\\mathfrak A}\\varepsilon^2$ when the activity norm and separately measured singleton coefficients are at most $\\varepsilon$. It preserves the common-shift period and all symmetries respected by the localizations. The linear map restricted to large supports has arbitrarily small operator norm, relative to the input activity norm, after choosing $\\mathfrak A$ sufficiently large at fixed $L$.*\n\n*For small field-even neutral bulk inputs, subtraction of the constant and quadratic gradient term gives $CL^{-3}$ before counting fine placements. Subtracting only the constant gives $CL^{-2}$ for even inputs and $CL^{-1}$ without evenness. There are respectively $O(L^2)$, $O(L)$, and $O(1)$ placements at a bulk block, a specified wall, and a fixed mark. At $\\alpha^2=8\\pi$, the fundamental charge has gain $O(L^{-2})$; for field-even bulk inputs, subtraction of its constant-background cosine coefficient gives an additional $L^{-1}$. Higher charges have summable stronger gains.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-inp-balanced-map",
        "exact_quote": "For the critical reference and small, even, translation- and square-covariant plane activities, the preceding coordinates satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{inp:parity-equations}\n u_+-u={}&-H_jZ^2+\n O\\bigl((|u|+Z^2)(Z^2+\\lVert B_e\\rVert)+\\lVert B_e\\rVert^2\n                    +\\lVert B_o\\rVert(|Z|+\\lVert B_o\\rVert)\\bigr),\\nonumber\\\\\n Z_+-Z={}&-b_juZ+\n O\\bigl((u^2+Z^2+\\lVert B_e\\rVert)|Z|\n       +( |u|+Z^2+\\lVert B_e\\rVert+\\lVert B_o\\rVert^2)\\lVert B_o\\rVert\\bigr),\\nonumber\\\\\n \\lVert B_{e,+}\\rVert\\le{}&\\theta_1\\lVert B_e\\rVert\n                              +C(|Z|+\\lVert B_o\\rVert)^2,\\nonumber\\\\\n \\lVert B_{o,+}\\rVert\\le{}&\\theta_1\\lVert B_o\\rVert\n                       +C(|u|+Z^2+\\lVert B_e\\rVert)|Z|,\n \\qquad \\theta_1<1.\n\\end{aligned}$$ The estimates include their differentiated analytic Taylor bounds. The coefficients are bounded above and away from zero, and $$\\label{inp:coefficient-limits}\n                         H_j\\to H>0,\\qquad b_j\\to2\\log L.$$ These are the map in Lemma 10.6 and the coefficient calculation in Proposition 8.5 of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)].",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The coefficients are bounded above and away from zero, and $$\\label{inp:coefficient-limits}\n                         H_j\\to H>0,\\qquad b_j\\to2\\log L.$$ These are the map in Lemma 10.6 and the coefficient calculation in Proposition 8.5 of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)].",

        "anchor": "stmt-prep-cutoff",
        "exact_quote": "On a compatible torus or free square of side $M$, the absolute change in the partition sum caused by the product of <a href=\"#prep:cutoff-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prep:cutoff-definition\">[prep:cutoff-definition]</a>, divided by the centered partition sum without cutoffs, is at most $$\\label{eq:cutoff-error}\n                         C(M/s)^2 e^{-c_1s}.$$ The assertion includes a free square with the integral connection $l$.         "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prep-lifts",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be the square minimizer at side $M$ and $r_\\infty$ the plane height just defined. If an axis rectangle and its fixed collar avoid the source, choose an integral gauge potential $g$ there with $\\partial g=l$. The local real lift is $\\lambda=(r+g)/\\alpha$, and $\\partial\\lambda=\\eta_l/\\alpha$. At interior supports meeting the source, the translation from the pierced-plane calculation to the square is $$\\label{eq:lift-difference}\n                         \\gamma_M=(r-r_\\infty)/\\alpha.$$ In lattice units, for the fixed derivative orders used in the maps, $$|\\nabla^a\\eta_l(x)|\\le C_a(1+\\operatorname{dist}(x,\\text{source}))^{-1-a}.$$ In an inner fixed fraction of the square, $$\\label{eq:lift-derivatives}\n                 |\\nabla^a\\gamma_M|\\le C_aM^{-a},\n                 \\qquad 1\\le a\\le5.$$ The connection estimate includes the reflected stencils at free walls. The affine contribution $(B_sr)_{c'}-(B_sr)_c+l_f$ to every retained observation difference is bounded uniformly in $s$ and $M$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-large-field",
        "exact_quote": "For every preparation graph $G$ and every real observation vector $y$, $$\\label{eq:large-observation}\n \\frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n       \\le\\exp\\left\\{-c\\sum_{\\substack{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)\\\\|d_f|\\le2T}}\n                                                    d_f^2\\right\\}.$$ Consequently, with a possibly smaller constant $c>0$ independent of $R,P,s$, $$\\label{prep:face-saving}\n \\frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n             \\prod_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}\\chi_T(d_f)\n                  \\le e^{-c\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}d_f^2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-large-field",
          "exact_quote": "For every preparation graph $G$ and every real observation vector $y$, $$\\label{eq:large-observation}\n \\frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n       \\le\\exp\\left\\{-c\\sum_{\\substack{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)\\\\|d_f|\\le2T}}\n                                                    d_f^2\\right\\}.$$ Consequently, with a possibly smaller constant $c>0$ independent of $R,P,s$, $$\\label{prep:face-saving}\n \\frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n             \\prod_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}\\chi_T(d_f)\n                  \\le e^{-c\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}d_f^2}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every preparation graph $G$ and every real observation vector $y$, $$\\label{eq:large-observation}\n \\frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n       \\le\\exp\\left\\{-c\\sum_{\\substack{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)\\\\|d_f|\\le2T}}\n                                                    d_f^2\\right\\}.$$ Consequently, with a possibly smaller constant $c>0$ independent of $R,P,s$, $$\\label{prep:face-saving}\n \\frac{Z_G^l(y)}{Z_G^0(0)}\n             \\prod_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}\\chi_T(d_f)\n                  \\le e^{-c\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}d_f^2}.$$",
      "explanation": "Tilted comparisons give quadratic suppression on controlled face differences, and the cutoff supplies suppression on the remaining faces. Their combination gives a strict large-observation saving.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "prep:cutoff-definition",
          "exact_quote": "On every observation face retained in the graph insert $$\\label{prep:cutoff-definition}\n \\chi_T(d_f)=\\int_{-T}^{T}\\frac{1}{\\sqrt{2\\pi}}\n                   e^{-(d_f-x)^2/2}\\,dx,\n \\qquad d_f=y_{c'}-y_c+l_f,\\qquad T=\\sqrt{s}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-localization",
        "exact_quote": "On each open preparation graph there are a quadratic form $L_G$ with a fixed-radius stencil in the faces, and a symmetric matrix $N_G$ on those faces, such that $$\\label{prep:shape-decomposition}\n E_G^l(y)=L_G(d)+\\sum_{f,g\\in\\mathcal F(G)}N_G(f,g)d_fd_g,$$ where $$\\label{prep:trial-energy}\n 0\\le L_G(d)\\le C\\sum_fd_f^2\n                       +C\\log(2s)\\sum_z\\kappa_z(d)^2.$$ The forms and matrices may be defined on arbitrary face arrays $d$, without imposing a curl constraint. Their nonlocal entries satisfy $$\\label{prep:matrix-decay}\n                         |N_G(f,g)|\\le Ce^{-c\\operatorname{dist}(f,g)}.$$ Their dependence on the graph is exponentially localized as well. In particular, if faces in a set $D$ are deleted, extend both matrices by zero on missing faces. With a fixed stencil enlargement understood in the distances, $$\\label{prep:matrix-cut}\n |(N_G-N_{G\\setminus D})(f,g)|\n \\le C\\exp\\left\\{-c\\left(\\operatorname{dist}(f,g)\n       +\\min\\{\\operatorname{dist}(f,D),\\operatorname{dist}(g,D)\\}\\right)\n                                                   \\right\\}.$$ The local stencils agree wherever the graphs agree. For the actual one-source data this implies $$\\label{eq:affine-shape-bound}\n E_G^l(y)\\le C\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}d_f^2\n                  +C\\log(2s)\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\text{pierced star present}\\}}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-localization",
          "exact_quote": "On each open preparation graph there are a quadratic form $L_G$ with a fixed-radius stencil in the faces, and a symmetric matrix $N_G$ on those faces, such that $$\\label{prep:shape-decomposition}\n E_G^l(y)=L_G(d)+\\sum_{f,g\\in\\mathcal F(G)}N_G(f,g)d_fd_g,$$ where $$\\label{prep:trial-energy}\n 0\\le L_G(d)\\le C\\sum_fd_f^2\n                       +C\\log(2s)\\sum_z\\kappa_z(d)^2.$$ The forms and matrices may be defined on arbitrary face arrays $d$, without imposing a curl constraint. Their nonlocal entries satisfy $$\\label{prep:matrix-decay}\n                         |N_G(f,g)|\\le Ce^{-c\\operatorname{dist}(f,g)}.$$ Their dependence on the graph is exponentially localized as well. In particular, if faces in a set $D$ are deleted, extend both matrices by zero on missing faces. With a fixed stencil enlargement understood in the distances, $$\\label{prep:matrix-cut}\n |(N_G-N_{G\\setminus D})(f,g)|\n \\le C\\exp\\left\\{-c\\left(\\operatorname{dist}(f,g)\n       +\\min\\{\\operatorname{dist}(f,D),\\operatorname{dist}(g,D)\\}\\right)\n                                                   \\right\\}.$$ The local stencils agree wherever the graphs agree.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-entry",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the regulator reserves as in <a href=\"#eq:entry-admissible-reserve\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:entry-admissible-reserve\">[eq:entry-admissible-reserve]</a>, and fix compatible parameters for the local maps of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)], including the block factor $L$, the support geometry, the activity weight $\\mathfrak A$, and the analytic radius. A fixed finite collection of stronger weights and entry-admissible regulator regimes may be specified. For every $\\epsilon>0$, one can choose a fixed dyadic $q$, an arbitrarily large dyadic $R$, and then $s_0$, such that the following conclusions hold for every compatible dyadic $s\\ge s_0$, with $P=R^5$ and $m=qRs$.*\n\n\n1.  *In the ordinary case, averaging the observation noise and the reference covariance below $m$ gives local activities $K_m$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:entry-small}\n                     \\lVert K_m\\rVert_{m,\\mathfrak A,V}\\le\\epsilon.$$ This holds on finite plane supports, on compatible tori, and on free squares whose sides are divisible by $sP$ and are at least $sP$. Up to a positive field-independent scalar, the corresponding subset partition function is exactly the averaged cutoff interaction. Activities with their required clearance from a wall or a torus identification are the corresponding plane activities. The construction is real, even, and covariant under the block translations and square symmetries that preserve the chosen hierarchy.*\n\n\n\n2.  *In a pierced free square in which the compulsory footprint and its required collars fit, the same scalar as in its ordinary counterpart may be extracted. The remaining partition function is $$\\label{eq:entry-linear-partition}\n                        \\mathcal Z_m^{(1)}(K_m^l,I_m;\\psi),$$ with exactly one insertion activity $I_m$. Its supports contain the mark block and the compulsory cover just specified. The background activities $K_m^l$ are arbitrarily small in their prescribed norms, and $$\\label{eq:entry-insertion}\n                        \\lVert I_m\\rVert_{m,\\mathfrak A,V}\n                             \\le C_R s^{C_R}.$$ Here and below $C_R$ may also depend on the fixed downstream norm and geometric parameters, but not on $s$ or on the square side. Ordinary occupied supports remain separated from the insertion by the original compatibility rule.*\n\n3.  *A support is called *shift-good* if its spanning rectangle, enlarged by $Hm$, misses the magnetic mark. The fixed constant $H$ is chosen from the local-map geometry. On such supports the pierced background is the ordinary background evaluated at the local lift $\\psi+\\lambda$, where $$\\partial\\lambda=\\eta_l/\\alpha.$$ On interior supports near the mark, including insertion supports, the activities are copies of the pierced-plane calculation evaluated at $\\psi+\\gamma_M$, with $\\gamma_M$ and its derivative bounds as in <a href=\"#eq:lift-difference\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lift-difference\">[eq:lift-difference]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:lift-derivatives\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lift-derivatives\">[eq:lift-derivatives]</a>. Copying always includes the collars and numerical dependence required by the activity.*\n\n*The assertions hold simultaneously in the fixed finite collection of reserve regimes. Their constants are uniform after the geometric parameters have been fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bulk-parity",
        "exact_quote": "The coordinate changes and parity-resolved maps of Proposition <a href=\"#inp:balanced-map\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"inp:balanced-map\">8</a> have a common analytic neighborhood and common bounds for all sufficiently large entry indices $i(J)$. At absolute index $k$, their two scalar quadratic coefficients obey $$\\label{eq:bulk-coefficient-rate}\n H_k=H+O((1+k)^{-2}),\\qquad\n b_k=b_*+O((1+k)^{-2}),\\qquad\n H>0,\\quad b_*=2\\log L.$$ The coefficients $H_k,b_k$ are independent of the entry index. The constants in the parity remainder bounds are uniform in the $J$-history.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bulk-physical-diagnostics",
        "exact_quote": "As $N\\to\\infty$ with $s/N\\to0$, locally uniformly for complex $w$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb Ee^{wX_N}&\\longrightarrow e^{agw^2/2},\n \\label{eq:bulk-physical-gradient}\\\\\n \\mathbb E(\\chi_Ne^{wX_N})-\\mathbb E\\chi_N\\,e^{agw^2/2}\n &\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:bulk-physical-phase}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All fixed source derivatives converge as well. For $s/N\\le P^{-1}$, the discrepancies can be made uniformly small by first increasing $R$, hence $P$, and then increasing $s$. The same statements hold with the cutoff on every stopping torus of a $J$-history, uniformly up to index $8J$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bulk-window-vanishing",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently small prescribed tube radius, the entry parameters can be chosen so that all $J$-histories, for sufficiently large $J$, remain in that tube through index $8J$. The stopped torus remainders remain in a fixed comparable tube. Moreover, for every fixed $0<c<8$, $$\\label{eq:bulk-window-vanishing}\n \\sup_{cJ\\le k\\le8J}\n \\bigl(|u_k^{(J)}|+|Z_k^{(J)}|\n       +\\lVert B_{e,k}^{(J)}\\rVert+\\lVert B_{o,k}^{(J)}\\rVert\\bigr)\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ The analogous stopped torus remainder quantities tend to zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bulk-window-vanishing",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently small prescribed tube radius, the entry parameters can be chosen so that all $J$-histories, for sufficiently large $J$, remain in that tube through index $8J$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bulk-odd",
        "exact_quote": "On every fixed window $cJ\\le k\\le8J$, $0<c<8$, and for large $J$, the period convention may be kept unchanged and $$\\label{eq:bulk-odd-estimates}\n Z_k^{(J)}>0,\\qquad -\\log Z_k^{(J)}=o(J),\\qquad\n \\lVert B_{e,k}^{(J)}\\rVert\\le C(Z_k^{(J)})^2,\n \\qquad\n \\lVert B_{o,k}^{(J)}\\rVert\\le\n C\\bigl(|u_k^{(J)}|+(Z_k^{(J)})^2\\bigr)Z_k^{(J)}.$$ The $o(J)$ and the constants are uniform on the window.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On every fixed window $cJ\\le k\\le8J$, $0<c<8$, and for large $J$, the period convention may be kept unchanged and $$\\label{eq:bulk-odd-estimates}\n Z_k^{(J)}>0,\\qquad -\\log Z_k^{(J)}=o(J),\\qquad\n \\lVert B_{e,k}^{(J)}\\rVert\\le C(Z_k^{(J)})^2,\n \\qquad\n \\lVert B_{o,k}^{(J)}\\rVert\\le\n C\\bigl(|u_k^{(J)}|+(Z_k^{(J)})^2\\bigr)Z_k^{(J)}.$$ The $o(J)$ and the constants are uniform on the window.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bulk-readouts",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<c<8$, uniformly on $cJ\\le k\\le8J$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n U_k&=\\frac{a}{1-u_k^{(J)}}+O((Z_k^{(J)})^2)+O(e^{-c'J}),\n \\label{eq:bulk-U-readout}\\\\\n Y_k&=M_kZ_k^{(J)}\n +O\\bigl((|u_k^{(J)}|+(Z_k^{(J)})^2)Z_k^{(J)}\\bigr)\n +O(e^{-c'J}).\n \\label{eq:bulk-Y-readout}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $c'>0$ may depend on $c$, and $M_k$ is bounded above and below by positive constants and is common to all histories, up to an exponentially small error which is included in the display. The coordinate changes also give $$\\label{eq:bulk-coordinate-conversion}\n t_k=u_k+O(u_k^2+Z_k^2),\\qquad\n z_k=\\ell_k^{-1}Z_k+O((|u_k|+Z_k^2)Z_k),\\qquad\n \\lVert\\mathcal R_k\\rVert\\le C(u_k^2+Z_k^2),$$ where $\\ell_k$ is the absolute-index fundamental balancing factor and is bounded above and below positively.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bulk-readouts",
          "exact_quote": "For every $0<c<8$, uniformly on $cJ\\le k\\le8J$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n U_k&=\\frac{a}{1-u_k^{(J)}}+O((Z_k^{(J)})^2)+O(e^{-c'J}),\n \\label{eq:bulk-U-readout}\\\\\n Y_k&=M_kZ_k^{(J)}\n +O\\bigl((|u_k^{(J)}|+(Z_k^{(J)})^2)Z_k^{(J)}\\bigr)\n +O(e^{-c'J}).\n \\label{eq:bulk-Y-readout}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $c'>0$ may depend on $c$, and $M_k$ is bounded above and below by positive constants and is common to all histories, up to an exponentially small error which is included in the display. The coordinate changes also give $$\\label{eq:bulk-coordinate-conversion}\n t_k=u_k+O(u_k^2+Z_k^2),\\qquad\n z_k=\\ell_k^{-1}Z_k+O((|u_k|+Z_k^2)Z_k),\\qquad\n \\lVert\\mathcal R_k\\rVert\\le C(u_k^2+Z_k^2),$$ where $\\ell_k$ is the absolute-index fundamental balancing factor and is bounded above and below positively.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $0<c<8$, uniformly on $cJ\\le k\\le8J$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n U_k&=\\frac{a}{1-u_k^{(J)}}+O((Z_k^{(J)})^2)+O(e^{-c'J}),\n \\label{eq:bulk-U-readout}\\\\\n Y_k&=M_kZ_k^{(J)}\n +O\\bigl((|u_k^{(J)}|+(Z_k^{(J)})^2)Z_k^{(J)}\\bigr)\n +O(e^{-c'J}).\n \\label{eq:bulk-Y-readout}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $c'>0$ may depend on $c$, and $M_k$ is bounded above and below by positive constants and is common to all histories, up to an exponentially small error which is included in the display. The coordinate changes also give $$\\label{eq:bulk-coordinate-conversion}\n t_k=u_k+O(u_k^2+Z_k^2),\\qquad\n z_k=\\ell_k^{-1}Z_k+O((|u_k|+Z_k^2)Z_k),\\qquad\n \\lVert\\mathcal R_k\\rVert\\le C(u_k^2+Z_k^2),$$ where $\\ell_k$ is the absolute-index fundamental balancing factor and is bounded above and below positively.",
      "explanation": "The same microscopic variance and mean-phase observables read the marginal coordinates of every history at a common absolute index. The phase multiplier is common and positive, while undoing the coordinate changes gives quadratic remainder control.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bulk-readouts",
          "exact_quote": "uniformly on $cJ\\le k\\le8J$,",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trajectory-sparse-splice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H,b>0$, let $j_0\\ge1$, and let $\\mathcal Q$ be a set of integers larger than $j_0$. Suppose that the increasing enumeration $(q_r)$ of $\\mathcal Q$ satisfies $$q_{r+1}\\ge \\rho q_r\n \\qquad\\text{for some fixed }\\rho>1.$$ The set $\\mathcal Q$ may also be finite or empty. For every integer $j\\ge j_0$, let $(u_j,Z_j)$ and $(\\widetilde u_{j+1},\\widetilde Z_{j+1})$ be real states with positive second coordinates. Assume that both coordinates of every state tend to zero as its scale index tends to infinity. Suppose that the ordinary evolution satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widetilde u_{j+1}-u_j\n   &=-H_j Z_j^2\n       +O\\bigl((|u_j|+Z_j^2)Z_j^2\\bigr),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-ordinary-u}\\\\\n \\frac{\\widetilde Z_{j+1}}{Z_j}\n   &=1-b_j u_j+O(u_j^2+Z_j^2),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-ordinary-z}\\\\\n H_j&=H+O(j^{-2}),\\qquad b_j=b+O(j^{-2}).\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-coefficients}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $q>j_0$ with $q\\notin\\mathcal Q$, assume $(u_q,Z_q)=(\\widetilde u_q,\\widetilde Z_q)$. At every $q\\in\\mathcal Q$, assume instead $$\\begin{aligned}\n |u_q-\\widetilde u_q|&\\le C\\widetilde Z_q^2,\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-jump-u}\\\\\n |Z_q-\\widetilde Z_q|\n   &\\le C\\bigl(|\\widetilde u_q|+\\widetilde Z_q^2\\bigr)\n                  \\widetilde Z_q.\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-jump-z}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All constants in these hypotheses are uniform in the scale index. Then, with $x_*=(H/b)^{1/2}$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac{u_j}{Z_j}&=x_*+O(j^{-1}),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-ratio}\\\\\n \\frac1{Z_j}&=b x_*j+O(\\log j),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-inverse}\\\\\n u_j&=\\frac1{bj}+O\\left(\\frac{\\log j}{j^2}\\right),\\qquad\n Z_j=\\frac1{\\sqrt{bH}\\,j}\n                 +O\\left(\\frac{\\log j}{j^2}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-asymptotics}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same estimates hold for the states immediately before jumps.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trajectory-overlap",
        "exact_quote": "Consider two bulk histories with comparable window parameters $J,J'$. At an index $j$ belonging to fixed positive-fraction windows of both histories, write $(u,Z)$ and $(u',Z')$ for their balanced marginal coordinates. For all sufficiently large indices, $$|u'-u|\\le CZ^2,\n \\qquad\n |Z'-Z|\\le C(|u|+Z^2)Z.\n \\label{eq:trajectory-overlap}$$ The constants are uniform when the comparison factors for the two windows are fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-trajectory-overlap",
          "exact_quote": "Consider two bulk histories with comparable window parameters $J,J'$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bulk-window",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the block factor $L$ and the other parameters chosen for the bulk maps. For every sufficiently large dyadic $J$, uniformly in the $J$-history at integer indices $J/2\\le j\\le3J$, its unbalanced gradient coordinate $t_j$, fundamental coordinate $z_j$, and plane remainder $\\mathcal R_j$ satisfy $$t_j=\\frac1{2j\\log L}\n                 +O\\left(\\frac{\\log j}{j^2}\\right),\n \\qquad |z_j|=O(j^{-1}),\n \\qquad \\|\\mathcal R_j\\|=O(j^{-2}).\n \\label{eq:trajectory-bulk-window}$$ The remainder norm is the unbalanced plane activity norm.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bulk-window",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the block factor $L$ and the other parameters chosen for the bulk maps.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-magnetic",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $j$, the exact maps from entry scale $i$ to stop $h$ can be chosen with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *At the stop, in the fixed norm regimes for the ordinary and defect activities, $$\\label{eq:mag-small-backgrounds}\n     \\max_{Q\\in\\{M,LM\\}}\n     \\bigl(\\lVert K^0_{h,Q}\\rVert+\\lVert K^l_{h,Q}\\rVert\\bigr)\\le \\frac{C}{j}.$$ The bulk copies satisfy the sharper conclusion of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:bulk-window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:bulk-window\">21</a>. On shift-good supports, pierced activities are exactly ordinary activities evaluated at the local lift. Interior pierced activities copy the plane calculation at the translate $\\gamma_Q$, whenever their required clearance holds.*\n\n\n\n2.  *Let $a^\\nu_{k,Q,b}$ be the designated scalar coefficient at coarse block $b$ in step $k$, for $\\nu=0,l$, and set $$\\label{eq:mag-background-products}\n     P^\\nu_Q=\\prod_{k=i}^{h-1}\\prod_b(1+a^\\nu_{k,Q,b}).$$ All factors are real and positive. The ratios agree exactly: $$\\label{eq:mag-common-background-ratio}\n     \\frac{P^l_M}{P^0_M}=\\frac{P^l_{LM}}{P^0_{LM}}=:\\rho_j.$$ For every fixed $\\epsilon_2>0$, the initial smallness choices can be made so that $|\\log\\rho_j|\\le\\epsilon_2j$ for all sufficiently large $j$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *There are a real scalar $\\sigma_Q$ and residual insertion activities $I_{h,Q}$ such that integration of the entry insertion over all shells below $v$ gives the exact field identity $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-identity}\n     \\mathbb E_{i:h}\\mathscr I_i(K^l_{i,Q},I_{i,Q};\\phi)\n     =P^l_Q\\left\\{\n        \\sigma_Q\\mathscr Z_h(K^l_{h,Q};\\phi)\n           +\\mathscr I_h(K^l_{h,Q},I_{h,Q};\\phi)\\right\\}.$$ Here $\\mathbb E_{i:h}$ denotes successive shell integration, with the remaining field $\\phi$ held fixed. The scalar is common to the two squares, $$\\label{eq:mag-common-insertion-scalar}\n     \\sigma_M=\\sigma_{LM}=: \\sigma_j,$$ and every support of $I_{h,Q}$ contains the designated mark block. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-bound}\n     \\lVert I_{h,Q}\\rVert\\le C_Rs^{C_R}L^{-2(h-i)/3},\n     \\qquad Q\\in\\{M,LM\\}.$$*\n\n*The products in <a href=\"#eq:mag-background-products\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mag-background-products\">[eq:mag-background-products]</a> contain only the RG extraction factors. They do not include the common entry scalar or the harmonic-energy factor of the Gaussian sector law.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-magnetic",

The ratios agree exactly: $$\\label{eq:mag-common-background-ratio}\n     \\frac{P^l_M}{P^0_M}=\\frac{P^l_{LM}}{P^0_{LM}}=:\\rho_j.$$ For every fixed $\\epsilon_2>0$, the initial smallness choices can be made so that $|\\log\\rho_j|\\le\\epsilon_2j$ for all sufficiently large $j$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *There are a real scalar $\\sigma_Q$ and residual insertion activities $I_{h,Q}$ such that integration of the entry insertion over all shells below $v$ gives the exact field identity $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-identity}\n     \\mathbb E_{i:h}\\mathscr I_i(K^l_{i,Q},I_{i,Q};\\phi)\n     =P^l_Q\\left\\{\n        \\sigma_Q\\mathscr Z_h(K^l_{h,Q};\\phi)\n           +\\mathscr I_h(K^l_{h,Q},I_{h,Q};\\phi)\\right\\}.$$ Here $\\mathbb E_{i:h}$ denotes successive shell integration, with the remaining field $\\phi$ held fixed. The scalar is common to the two squares, $$\\label{eq:mag-common-insertion-scalar}\n     \\sigma_M=\\sigma_{LM}=: \\sigma_j,$$ and every support of $I_{h,Q}$ contains the designated mark block. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-bound}\n     \\lVert I_{h,Q}\\rVert\\le C_Rs^{C_R}L^{-2(h-i)/3},\n     \\qquad Q\\in\\{M,LM\\}.$$*\n\n*The products in <a href=\"#eq:mag-background-products\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mag-background-products\">[eq:mag-background-products]</a> contain only the RG extraction factors.       "exact_statement": "For all sufficiently large $j$, the exact maps from entry scale $i$ to stop $h$ can be chosen with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *At the stop, in the fixed norm regimes for the ordinary and defect activities, $$\\label{eq:mag-small-backgrounds}\n     \\max_{Q\\in\\{M,LM\\}}\n     \\bigl(\\lVert K^0_{h,Q}\\rVert+\\lVert K^l_{h,Q}\\rVert\\bigr)\\le \\frac{C}{j}.$$ The bulk copies satisfy the sharper conclusion of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:bulk-window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:bulk-window\">21</a>. On shift-good supports, pierced activities are exactly ordinary activities evaluated at the local lift. Interior pierced activities copy the plane calculation at the translate $\\gamma_Q$, whenever their required clearance holds.*\n\n\n\n2.  *Let $a^\\nu_{k,Q,b}$ be the designated scalar coefficient at coarse block $b$ in step $k$, for $\\nu=0,l$, and set $$\\label{eq:mag-background-products}\n     P^\\nu_Q=\\prod_{k=i}^{h-1}\\prod_b(1+a^\\nu_{k,Q,b}).$$ All factors are real and positive. The ratios agree exactly: $$\\label{eq:mag-common-background-ratio}\n     \\frac{P^l_M}{P^0_M}=\\frac{P^l_{LM}}{P^0_{LM}}=:\\rho_j.$$ For every fixed $\\epsilon_2>0$, the initial smallness choices can be made so that $|\\log\\rho_j|\\le\\epsilon_2j$ for all sufficiently large $j$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *There are a real scalar $\\sigma_Q$ and residual insertion activities $I_{h,Q}$ such that integration of the entry insertion over all shells below $v$ gives the exact field identity $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-identity}\n     \\mathbb E_{i:h}\\mathscr I_i(K^l_{i,Q},I_{i,Q};\\phi)\n     =P^l_Q\\left\\{\n        \\sigma_Q\\mathscr Z_h(K^l_{h,Q};\\phi)\n           +\\mathscr I_h(K^l_{h,Q},I_{h,Q};\\phi)\\right\\}.$$ Here $\\mathbb E_{i:h}$ denotes successive shell integration, with the remaining field $\\phi$ held fixed. The scalar is common to the two squares, $$\\label{eq:mag-common-insertion-scalar}\n     \\sigma_M=\\sigma_{LM}=: \\sigma_j,$$ and every support of $I_{h,Q}$ contains the designated mark block. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-bound}\n     \\lVert I_{h,Q}\\rVert\\le C_Rs^{C_R}L^{-2(h-i)/3},\n     \\qquad Q\\in\\{M,LM\\}.$$*\n\n*The products in <a href=\"#eq:mag-background-products\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mag-background-products\">[eq:mag-background-products]</a> contain only the RG extraction factors. They do not include the common entry scalar or the harmonic-energy factor of the Gaussian sector law.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **mass-defined critical threshold** ([source](#eq:intro-threshold)): $$\label{eq:intro-threshold} C_b(r)=\lim_{R\to\infty}\mathbb E^{\mathrm{free}}_{R,b} \cos(\theta_0-\theta_{re_1}),\qquad m(b)=\lim_{r\to\infty}-\frac{1}{r}\log C_b(r),\qquad b_c=\inf\{b>0:m(b)=0\}.$$ The existence of these limits and the fact that $0<b_c<\infty$ with $m(b_c)=0$ are among the inputs recalled in Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a>.
- **aligned-boundary center magnetization** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Our observable is $$a_n=\mathbb E^0_{n,b_c}\cos\theta_0.$$
- **integral connection** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An integral connection $l$ is an edge cochain with values in $2\pi\mathbb Z$; it replaces $\partial h$ by $\partial h+l$ in the weights.
- **height partition ratio** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Write $Z_G^l$ for the resulting sum and $F_G^l=Z_G^l/Z_G^0$.
- **primary unit-edge Laplacian** ([source](#inp:energy-convention)): $$\label{inp:energy-convention} \langle v,A_Gv\rangle=\sum_{\{x,y\}\in E(G)}(v_y-v_x)^2.$$ Thus each undirected edge contributes once. Heights take values in $2\pi\mathbb Z$, and an edge difference $2\pi k$ has weight $$p_b(k)=e^{-b}I_k(b),\qquad \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}p_b(k)e^{tk}=\exp\{b(\cosh t-1)\}.$$ An integral connection $l$ is an edge cochain with values in $2\pi\mathbb Z$; it replaces $\partial h$ by $\partial h+l$ in the weights. Write $Z_G^l$ for the resulting sum and $F_G^l=Z_G^l/Z_G^0$. Without observations or absolute pins, a constant translation by $2\pi\mathbb Z$ is divided out on each free component. If observations confine the constants, their translation copies are summed instead. Changing $l$ by an integral gradient is an exact change of height variables.
- **structural height coefficient** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The structural coefficient is the limit $$a(b)=\lim_{m\to\infty} \operatorname{Var}_{B_m,b}\langle h,A_{B_m}g_m\rangle.$$
- **polynomial low-pass covariances** ([source](#inp:covariances)): $$\label{inp:covariances} C_{<u}=A^{-1}(1-\mathsf P_u),\qquad \Gamma_u=A^{-1}(\mathsf P_u-\mathsf P_{Lu}),\qquad C_{\ge u}=A^+\mathsf P_u,$$ with $C_{<m}$ integrated at entry. The first two inverses mean polynomial continuation at the zero eigenvalue; the last formula acts on nonconstant modes. No independent-variance factor is inserted. The same polynomials are used on free squares, with even reflection at their walls. Positivity, finite range, and exact local copying apply to the polynomial accumulated and shell covariances: their ranges are $O(u)$ and $O(Lu)$, respectively. The terminal covariance need not have finite range. These assertions and the derivative bounds follow from (OpenAI 2026a, Lemmas 8.1 and 4.1) [[B](#ref-B)] with this argument. In particular positive-order shell and terminal estimates through the orders used below hold uniformly when $u\le M/D_0$, with fixed sufficiently large $D_0$; the plane shell diagonal is $(2\pi)^{-1}\log L$ with an error that is a negative power of $u$.
- **connected polymer** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A polymer $X$ is a nonempty finite block set connected by steps of sup block-index distance at most $\rho$; $\|X\|$ counts its blocks.
- **analytic point norm** ([source](#inp:point-norm)): $$\label{inp:point-norm} \|F\|_{u,X,\psi}= \sum_{r\ge0}\frac{h_0^r}{r!} \sup_{\lVert f_i\rVert_{u,D_u(X)}\le1} \|D^rF(\psi)[f_1,\ldots,f_r]\|.$$ The term with $r=0$ is $\|F(\psi)\|$. These real derivatives have locally convergent analytic extensions. The full derivative sum controls complex translations of test norm less than $h_0$ and obeys the product inequality.
- **homogeneous observation energy** ([source](#inp:observation-energy)): The entry construction needs one additional, homogeneous quadratic regulator. Let $B_s$ average over disjoint side-$s$ observation cells. On an open union $D$ of whole cells define $$\label{inp:observation-energy} E_D(y)=\inf_v\left\{\alpha^{-2}\langle v,A_Dv\rangle +\lVert B_sv-y\rVert^2\right\},$$ with free constants on every component. Here $A_D$ retains only internal bonds; it differs at artificial boundaries from the half-edge allocation $g_D$. Choose constants $1<p_1<p_2$ and $0<h_*<1/p_2$, with the strict reserves required by the entry estimates. At scale $u=mL^j$, put $$\label{inp:variational-regulator} V_j(X,\psi)=\frac12\sup_{\zeta,e} \left\{h_*E_D\bigl(B_s\alpha(\psi+\zeta)+e\bigr) -p_1^{-1}\bigl(\lVert\zeta\rVert_{<u}^2+\lVert e\rVert^2\bigr)\right\}.$$ The Cameron norm $\lVert\zeta\rVert_{<u}$ belongs to the ambient accumulated covariance $C_{<u}$, including the pieces integrated before entry. Only its restriction to $D$ enters the supremum. The variable $e$ is the independent unit observation noise, included once. For a singular covariance we use its Gaussian Hilbert space, without inverting a null direction.
- **variational observation regulator** ([source](#inp:variational-regulator)): The entry construction needs one additional, homogeneous quadratic regulator. Let $B_s$ average over disjoint side-$s$ observation cells. On an open union $D$ of whole cells define $$\label{inp:observation-energy} E_D(y)=\inf_v\left\{\alpha^{-2}\langle v,A_Dv\rangle +\lVert B_sv-y\rVert^2\right\},$$ with free constants on every component. Here $A_D$ retains only internal bonds; it differs at artificial boundaries from the half-edge allocation $g_D$. Choose constants $1<p_1<p_2$ and $0<h_*<1/p_2$, with the strict reserves required by the entry estimates. At scale $u=mL^j$, put $$\label{inp:variational-regulator} V_j(X,\psi)=\frac12\sup_{\zeta,e} \left\{h_*E_D\bigl(B_s\alpha(\psi+\zeta)+e\bigr) -p_1^{-1}\bigl(\lVert\zeta\rVert_{<u}^2+\lVert e\rVert^2\bigr)\right\}.$$ The Cameron norm $\lVert\zeta\rVert_{<u}$ belongs to the ambient accumulated covariance $C_{<u}$, including the pieces integrated before entry. Only its restriction to $D$ enters the supremum. The variable $e$ is the independent unit observation noise, included once. For a singular covariance we use its Gaussian Hilbert space, without inverting a null direction.
- **regulated analytic activity norm** ([source](#inp:norm)): For a polymer activity $K$ our norm is $$\label{inp:norm} \lVert K\rVert_{u,\mathfrak A,V}= \sup_{X,\psi} \frac{\mathfrak A^{\|X\|}\|K(X)\|_{u,X,\psi}} {W_u^\kappa(X,\psi)e^{V_j(X,\psi)}}.$$ The supremum includes all real fields. The activity weight $\mathfrak A$ is unrelated to the Laplacian $A$. A fixed finite collection of stronger weights, slightly larger regulator coefficients, and analytic-radius reserves may be chosen throughout.
- **compatible partition functional** ([source](#inp:partition-function)): $$\label{inp:partition-function} \mathcal Z_u(K;\psi)= \sum_{\mathcal C\ \mathrm{compatible}}\prod_{X\in\mathcal C}K(X;\psi),$$ including the empty collection with value one. Equivalently one sums over block subsets and multiplies the activities of their $\rho$-connected components. A compulsory insertion activity $I$ uses the degree-one functional $$\label{inp:insertion-partition} \mathcal Z_u^{(1)}(K,I;\psi)= \sum_X I(X;\psi) \sum_{\substack{\mathcal C\ \mathrm{compatible}\\ Y\ \mathrm{compatible\ with}\ X\ (Y\in\mathcal C)}} \prod_{Y\in\mathcal C}K(Y;\psi).$$ It is the coefficient of $\tau$ in $\mathcal Z_u(K+\tau I;\psi)$, with $\tau^2=0$. An input polymer is called small when $\|X\|\le n_0$, and large when $\|X\|>n_0$, with the geometric threshold fixed above.
- **compulsory insertion functional** ([source](#inp:insertion-partition)): $$\label{inp:insertion-partition} \mathcal Z_u^{(1)}(K,I;\psi)= \sum_X I(X;\psi) \sum_{\substack{\mathcal C\ \mathrm{compatible}\\ Y\ \mathrm{compatible\ with}\ X\ (Y\in\mathcal C)}} \prod_{Y\in\mathcal C}K(Y;\psi).$$ It is the coefficient of $\tau$ in $\mathcal Z_u(K+\tau I;\psi)$, with $\tau^2=0$. An input polymer is called small when $\|X\|\le n_0$, and large when $\|X\|>n_0$, with the geometric threshold fixed above.
- **balanced marginal coordinates** ([source](#inp:balance)): $$\begin{aligned} \label{inp:balance} T&=t+\sum_{h\ge j}P_h^tC_{h:j}\mathcal R,\nonumber\\ Z&=\ell_jz+\sum_{h\ge j}\ell_{h+1}P_h^zC_{h:j}\mathcal R. \end{aligned}$$ Both projection series have geometric tails. Their linear map is $(T,Z,\mathcal R)\mapsto(T,Z,C_j\mathcal R)$.
- **affine Gaussian shape energy** ([source](#eq:shape-energy)): Let $G$ be a graph obtained by the cell cuts just described. Write $\mathcal F(G)$ for its retained observation faces. Free constants are allowed on every connected component. Define the Gaussian shape energy by $$\label{eq:shape-energy} E_G^l(y)=\inf_v\left\{\alpha^{-2}\|\partial v+l\|_G^2 +\|B_sv-y\|^2\right\}.$$ Write $E_G=E_G^0$. Let $Z_G^l(y)$ be the Bessel height sum with observation penalty $\exp(-\|B_sh-y\|^2/2)$ and with all component constants summed. No cutoff occurs in this definition. More explicitly, apart from the common normalization of the observation noise, it is $$Z_G^l(y)=\sum_{h\in(2\pi\mathbb Z)^{V(G)}} \prod_{e\in E(G)}p_{b_c}\!\left(\frac{\partial h(e)+l(e)}{2\pi}\right) e^{-\|B_sh-y\|^2/2}.$$ Here $E(G)$ denotes the set of microscopic edges, each counted once; the observation penalty makes the sum over each component constant finite. When subsequently integrating observations, one divides out common translations, or uses one period for each free constant.
- **cutoff interaction** ([source](#eq:interaction)): For clarity, define the interaction on every cut graph by $$\label{eq:interaction} J_G^l(y)=e^{E_G^l(y)/2}Z_G^l(y) \prod_{f\in\mathcal F(G)}\chi_T(d_f).$$ On the full graph suppress $G$. Relative to the unpierced Gaussian integral, evaluating $J^l$ under <a href="#eq:gaussian-observation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gaussian-observation">[eq:gaussian-observation]</a> introduces the scalar $$\label{prep:magnetic-scalar} e^{-\|\eta_l\|^2/(2\alpha^2)}.$$ To see that this is the complete scalar, the Gaussian integral with fixed observations equals a common determinant factor times $e^{-E^l(y)/2}$. Translating by $r$ makes its determinant exactly the unpierced determinant and leaves the constant energy $\|\eta_l\|^2/\alpha^2$. Multiplication by <a href="#eq:interaction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:interaction">[eq:interaction]</a> cancels the observation shape. Thus no additional factor involving a chosen local harmonic lift is present.
- **shift-good support** ([source](#stmt-prop-entry)): (Mesoscopic analytic entry). *Choose the regulator reserves as in <a href="#eq:entry-admissible-reserve" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:entry-admissible-reserve">[eq:entry-admissible-reserve]</a>, and fix compatible parameters for the local maps of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)], including the block factor $L$, the support geometry, the activity weight $\mathfrak A$, and the analytic radius. A fixed finite collection of stronger weights and entry-admissible regulator regimes may be specified. For every $\epsilon>0$, one can choose a fixed dyadic $q$, an arbitrarily large dyadic $R$, and then $s_0$, such that the following conclusions hold for every compatible dyadic $s\ge s_0$, with $P=R^5$ and $m=qRs$.* 1. *In the ordinary case, averaging the observation noise and the reference covariance below $m$ gives local activities $K_m$ satisfying $$\label{eq:entry-small} \lVert K_m\rVert_{m,\mathfrak A,V}\le\epsilon.$$ This holds on finite plane supports, on compatible tori, and on free squares whose sides are divisible by $sP$ and are at least $sP$. Up to a positive field-independent scalar, the corresponding subset partition function is exactly the averaged cutoff interaction. Activities with their required clearance from a wall or a torus identification are the corresponding plane activities. The construction is real, even, and covariant under the block translations and square symmetries that preserve the chosen hierarchy.* 2. *In a pierced free square in which the compulsory footprint and its required collars fit, the same scalar as in its ordinary counterpart may be extracted. The remaining partition function is $$\label{eq:entry-linear-partition} \mathcal Z_m^{(1)}(K_m^l,I_m;\psi),$$ with exactly one insertion activity $I_m$. Its supports contain the mark block and the compulsory cover just specified. The background activities $K_m^l$ are arbitrarily small in their prescribed norms, and $$\label{eq:entry-insertion} \lVert I_m\rVert_{m,\mathfrak A,V} \le C_R s^{C_R}.$$ Here and below $C_R$ may also depend on the fixed downstream norm and geometric parameters, but not on $s$ or on the square side. Ordinary occupied supports remain separated from the insertion by the original compatibility rule.* 3. *A support is called *shift-good* if its spanning rectangle, enlarged by $Hm$, misses the magnetic mark.
- **common microscopic readouts** ([source](#eq:bulk-common-observables)): $$\label{eq:bulk-common-observables} U_k=\frac{\operatorname{Var}(\widetilde X_{N_k})}{g}, \qquad Y_k=\mathbb Ee^{i\bar h_{N_k}}.$$ Both quantities depend only on the absolute index $k$.

          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a graph obtained by the cell cuts just described. Write $\\mathcal F(G)$ for its retained observation faces. More explicitly, apart from the common normalization of the observation noise, it is $$Z_G^l(y)=\\sum_{h\\in(2\\pi\\mathbb Z)^{V(G)}}\n       \\prod_{e\\in E(G)}p_{b_c}\\!\\left(\\frac{\\partial h(e)+l(e)}{2\\pi}\\right)\n                         e^{-|B_sh-y|^2/2}.$$ Here $E(G)$ denotes the set of microscopic edges, each counted once; the observation penalty makes the sum over each component constant finite.           "anchor": "stmt-prop-entry",
          "exact_quote": "A support is called *shift-good* if its spanning rectangle, enlarged by $Hm$, misses the magnetic mark.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "(Mesoscopic analytic entry). *Choose the regulator reserves as in <a href=\"#eq:entry-admissible-reserve\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:entry-admissible-reserve\">[eq:entry-admissible-reserve]</a>, and fix compatible parameters for the local maps of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)], including the block factor $L$, the support geometry, the activity weight $\\mathfrak A$, and the analytic radius. A fixed finite collection of stronger weights and entry-admissible regulator regimes may be specified. For every $\\epsilon>0$, one can choose a fixed dyadic $q$, an arbitrarily large dyadic $R$, and then $s_0$, such that the following conclusions hold for every compatible dyadic $s\\ge s_0$, with $P=R^5$ and $m=qRs$.* 1. *In the ordinary case, averaging the observation noise and the reference covariance below $m$ gives local activities $K_m$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:entry-small} \\lVert K_m\\rVert_{m,\\mathfrak A,V}\\le\\epsilon.$$ This holds on finite plane supports, on compatible tori, and on free squares whose sides are divisible by $sP$ and are at least $sP$. Up to a positive field-independent scalar, the corresponding subset partition function is exactly the averaged cutoff interaction. Activities with their required clearance from a wall or a torus identification are the corresponding plane activities. The construction is real, even, and covariant under the block translations and square symmetries that preserve the chosen hierarchy.* 2. *In a pierced free square in which the compulsory footprint and its required collars fit, the same scalar as in its ordinary counterpart may be extracted. The remaining partition function is $$\\label{eq:entry-linear-partition} \\mathcal Z_m^{(1)}(K_m^l,I_m;\\psi),$$ with exactly one insertion activity $I_m$. Its supports contain the mark block and the compulsory cover just specified. The background activities $K_m^l$ are arbitrarily small in their prescribed norms, and $$\\label{eq:entry-insertion} \\lVert I_m\\rVert_{m,\\mathfrak A,V} \\le C_R s^{C_R}.$$ Here and below $C_R$ may also depend on the fixed downstream norm and geometric parameters, but not on $s$ or on the square side. Ordinary occupied supports remain separated from the insertion by the original compatibility rule.* 3. *A support is called *shift-good* if its spanning rectangle, enlarged by $Hm$, misses the magnetic mark.",

      "name": "shift-good support",
      "scope": "entry activities"
    },
