| q-shape-correction-matrix-decay | \|N_G(f,g)\| | ≤ | $Ce^{-c\operatorname{dist}(f,g)}$ | Uniform exponential localization in observation-cell distance. |
| q-entry-background-smallness | \lVert K_m\rVert_{m,\mathfrak A,V} | ≤ | $\epsilon$ | Geometry and map parameters precede the observation refinement; a computable refinement threshold is ABSENT. |
| q-entry-insertion-polynomial-bound | \lVert I_m\rVert_{m,\mathfrak A,V} | ≤ | $C_R s^{C_R}$ | Constants may depend on fixed tile and downstream parameters, but not on observation side or square side. |
| q-absolute-gradient-quadratic-coefficient | H_k | = | $H+O((1+k)^{-2})$ | The positive limiting coefficient and the absolute-index sequence are independent of entry. |
| q-absolute-fundamental-quadratic-coefficient | b_k | = | $b_*+O((1+k)^{-2})$ | The limiting coefficient is fixed by the chosen block factor. |
| q-limiting-fundamental-coefficient | b_* | = | $2\log L$ | Absolute-index coefficient limit. |
| q-sparse-splice-gradient-asymptotic | u_j | = | $\frac1{bj}+O\left(\frac{\log j}{j^2}\right)$ | Jumps are geometrically separated; the same estimate covers pre-jump states. |
| q-bulk-gradient-asymptotic | t_j | = | $\frac1{2j\log L} +O\left(\frac{\log j}{j^2}\right)$ | Uniform overlap-window asymptotic with fixed map parameters. |
| q-bulk-plane-remainder | \\|\mathcal R_j\\| | = | $O(j^{-2})$ | The bound concerns the remainder after the running singleton coordinates are separated. |
| q-residual-insertion-contraction | \lVert I_{h,Q}\rVert | ≤ | $C_Rs^{C_R}L^{-2(h-i)/3}$ | The insertion remains linear and the background stays in fixed small norm regimes. |
| q-terminal-partition-log-remainder | Higher-degree terminal logarithm error | = | $O_L(D^4/j^2)$ | Fixed regulator reserves; terminal block ratio follows the common stopping rule. |
| q-deterministic-energy-increment | E_{LM}-E_M | = | $2\pi\log L+O_L(n^{-c_4})$ | Fixed enlargement factor and growing square size. |
| q-summable-magnetization-log-increment | \log a_{n_{j+1}}-\log a_{n_j} | = | $-\frac{\log L}{8}+\frac1{16j} +O_L(j^{-1-\epsilon})$ | The logarithmic increment error is absolutely summable; an explicit uniform finite-size acceptance threshold is ABSENT. |

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-entry",
          "exact_quote": "Choose the regulator reserves as in <a href=\"#eq:entry-admissible-reserve\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:entry-admissible-reserve\">[eq:entry-admissible-reserve]</a>, and fix compatible parameters for the local maps of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)], including the block factor $L$, the support geometry, the activity weight $\\mathfrak A$, and the analytic radius. A fixed finite collection of stronger weights and entry-admissible regulator regimes may be specified. For every $\\epsilon>0$, one can choose a fixed dyadic $q$, an arbitrarily large dyadic $R$, and then $s_0$, such that the following conclusions hold for every compatible dyadic $s\\ge s_0$, with $P=R^5$ and $m=qRs$.*\n\n\n1.  *In the ordinary case, averaging the observation noise and the reference covariance below $m$ gives local activities $K_m$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:entry-small}\n                     \\lVert K_m\\rVert_{m,\\mathfrak A,V}\\le\\epsilon.$$ This holds on finite plane supports, on compatible tori, and on free squares whose sides are divisible by $sP$ and are at least $sP$. Up to a positive field-independent scalar, the corresponding subset partition function is exactly the averaged cutoff interaction. Activities with their required clearance from a wall or a torus identification are the corresponding plane activities. The construction is real, even, and covariant under the block translations and square symmetries that preserve the chosen hierarchy.*\n\n\n\n2.  *In a pierced free square in which the compulsory footprint and its required collars fit, the same scalar as in its ordinary counterpart may be extracted. The remaining partition function is $$\\label{eq:entry-linear-partition}\n                        \\mathcal Z_m^{(1)}(K_m^l,I_m;\\psi),$$ with exactly one insertion activity $I_m$. Its supports contain the mark block and the compulsory cover just specified. The background activities $K_m^l$ are arbitrarily small in their prescribed norms, and $$\\label{eq:entry-insertion}\n                        \\lVert I_m\\rVert_{m,\\mathfrak A,V}\n                             \\le C_R s^{C_R}.$$ Here and below $C_R$ may also depend on the fixed downstream norm and geometric parameters, but not on $s$ or on the square side. Ordinary occupied supports remain separated from the insertion by the original compatibility rule.*\n\n3.  *A support is called *shift-good* if its spanning rectangle, enlarged by $Hm$, misses the magnetic mark. The fixed constant $H$ is chosen from the local-map geometry. On such supports the pierced background is the ordinary background evaluated at the local lift $\\psi+\\lambda$, where $$\\partial\\lambda=\\eta_l/\\alpha.$$ On interior supports near the mark, including insertion supports, the activities are copies of the pierced-plane calculation evaluated at $\\psi+\\gamma_M$, with $\\gamma_M$ and its derivative bounds as in <a href=\"#eq:lift-difference\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lift-difference\">[eq:lift-difference]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:lift-derivatives\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lift-derivatives\">[eq:lift-derivatives]</a>. Copying always includes the collars and numerical dependence required by the activity.*\n\n*The assertions hold simultaneously in the fixed finite collection of reserve regimes. Their constants are uniform after the geometric parameters have been fixed.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-trajectory-sparse-splice",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H,b>0$, let $j_0\\ge1$, and let $\\mathcal Q$ be a set of integers larger than $j_0$. Suppose that the increasing enumeration $(q_r)$ of $\\mathcal Q$ satisfies $$q_{r+1}\\ge \\rho q_r\n \\qquad\\text{for some fixed }\\rho>1.$$ The set $\\mathcal Q$ may also be finite or empty. For every integer $j\\ge j_0$, let $(u_j,Z_j)$ and $(\\widetilde u_{j+1},\\widetilde Z_{j+1})$ be real states with positive second coordinates. Assume that both coordinates of every state tend to zero as its scale index tends to infinity. Suppose that the ordinary evolution satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widetilde u_{j+1}-u_j\n   &=-H_j Z_j^2\n       +O\\bigl((|u_j|+Z_j^2)Z_j^2\\bigr),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-ordinary-u}\\\\\n \\frac{\\widetilde Z_{j+1}}{Z_j}\n   &=1-b_j u_j+O(u_j^2+Z_j^2),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-ordinary-z}\\\\\n H_j&=H+O(j^{-2}),\\qquad b_j=b+O(j^{-2}).\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-coefficients}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $q>j_0$ with $q\\notin\\mathcal Q$, assume $(u_q,Z_q)=(\\widetilde u_q,\\widetilde Z_q)$. At every $q\\in\\mathcal Q$, assume instead $$\\begin{aligned}\n |u_q-\\widetilde u_q|&\\le C\\widetilde Z_q^2,\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-jump-u}\\\\\n |Z_q-\\widetilde Z_q|\n   &\\le C\\bigl(|\\widetilde u_q|+\\widetilde Z_q^2\\bigr)\n                  \\widetilde Z_q.\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-jump-z}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All constants in these hypotheses are uniform in the scale index. Then, with $x_*=(H/b)^{1/2}$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac{u_j}{Z_j}&=x_*+O(j^{-1}),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-ratio}\\\\\n \\frac1{Z_j}&=b x_*j+O(\\log j),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-inverse}\\\\\n u_j&=\\frac1{bj}+O\\left(\\frac{\\log j}{j^2}\\right),\\qquad\n Z_j=\\frac1{\\sqrt{bH}\\,j}\n                 +O\\left(\\frac{\\log j}{j^2}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-asymptotics}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same estimates hold for the states immediately before jumps.",

        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-magnetic",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $j$, the exact maps from entry scale $i$ to stop $h$ can be chosen with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *At the stop, in the fixed norm regimes for the ordinary and defect activities, $$\\label{eq:mag-small-backgrounds}\n     \\max_{Q\\in\\{M,LM\\}}\n     \\bigl(\\lVert K^0_{h,Q}\\rVert+\\lVert K^l_{h,Q}\\rVert\\bigr)\\le \\frac{C}{j}.$$ The bulk copies satisfy the sharper conclusion of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:bulk-window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:bulk-window\">21</a>. On shift-good supports, pierced activities are exactly ordinary activities evaluated at the local lift. Interior pierced activities copy the plane calculation at the translate $\\gamma_Q$, whenever their required clearance holds.*\n\n\n\n2.  *Let $a^\\nu_{k,Q,b}$ be the designated scalar coefficient at coarse block $b$ in step $k$, for $\\nu=0,l$, and set $$\\label{eq:mag-background-products}\n     P^\\nu_Q=\\prod_{k=i}^{h-1}\\prod_b(1+a^\\nu_{k,Q,b}).$$ All factors are real and positive. The ratios agree exactly: $$\\label{eq:mag-common-background-ratio}\n     \\frac{P^l_M}{P^0_M}=\\frac{P^l_{LM}}{P^0_{LM}}=:\\rho_j.$$ For every fixed $\\epsilon_2>0$, the initial smallness choices can be made so that $|\\log\\rho_j|\\le\\epsilon_2j$ for all sufficiently large $j$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *There are a real scalar $\\sigma_Q$ and residual insertion activities $I_{h,Q}$ such that integration of the entry insertion over all shells below $v$ gives the exact field identity $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-identity}\n     \\mathbb E_{i:h}\\mathscr I_i(K^l_{i,Q},I_{i,Q};\\phi)\n     =P^l_Q\\left\\{\n        \\sigma_Q\\mathscr Z_h(K^l_{h,Q};\\phi)\n           +\\mathscr I_h(K^l_{h,Q},I_{h,Q};\\phi)\\right\\}.$$ Here $\\mathbb E_{i:h}$ denotes successive shell integration, with the remaining field $\\phi$ held fixed. The scalar is common to the two squares, $$\\label{eq:mag-common-insertion-scalar}\n     \\sigma_M=\\sigma_{LM}=: \\sigma_j,$$ and every support of $I_{h,Q}$ contains the designated mark block. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:mag-insertion-bound}\n     \\lVert I_{h,Q}\\rVert\\le C_Rs^{C_R}L^{-2(h-i)/3},\n     \\qquad Q\\in\\{M,LM\\}.$$*\n\n*The products in <a href=\"#eq:mag-background-products\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mag-background-products\">[eq:mag-background-products]</a> contain only the RG extraction factors. They do not include the common entry scalar or the harmonic-energy factor of the Gaussian sector law.",

        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-fixed-boundary-duality** [claim](#stmt-inp-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inp-duality): Fourier current expansion, a center-to-boundary current subtraction, and rotation to a dual height gradient. -> Positive affine height partition representation of center magnetization.
- **step-finite-comparison-inputs** [claim](#stmt-inp-comparisons) / [proof](#stmt-inp-comparisons): Use the stated companion finite-graph comparisons, including independent Gaussian observation noises. -> Covariance, transform, characteristic, and shifted-ratio comparison inputs.
- **step-height-limit-inputs** [claim](#stmt-inp-height) / [proof](#stmt-inp-height): Use the stated critical coefficient and mixed-geometry height and pin limits. -> Critical Gaussian coefficient and the continuum observation and pin comparisons.
- **step-center-comparison** [claim](#stmt-inp-regular-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inp-regular-variation): Compare identical centered annular nests, cancel their common core and regular Green term, and use the positive core for the lower bound. -> Crude magnetization lower bound and sequential dilation ratio.
- **step-regulator-input** [claim](#stmt-inp-regulator) / [proof](#stmt-inp-regulator): Use homogeneous observation-energy contraction and the stated shell and terminal regulator input. -> Uniform regulated integration and local-map bounds.
- **step-exact-partition-map** [claim](#stmt-inp-partition-map) / [proof](#stmt-inp-partition-map): Use exact compatible partition algebra, designated local subtractions, and the companion analytic estimates. -> Exact scalar-times-partition identity and localization gains.
- **step-balanced-parity-input** [claim](#stmt-inp-balanced-map) / [proof](#stmt-inp-balanced-map): Apply future linear projections and subtract the exact Gaussian trajectory before splitting half-period parity. -> Balanced marginal recurrences with parity-resolved remainders.
- **step-cutoff-error** [claim](#stmt-prep-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-cutoff): Use bounded-cost adjacent-cell flows, tilted comparison, exponential tails, and a face union bound. -> Exponentially small centered-normalized cutoff error.
- **step-local-lifts** [claim](#stmt-prep-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-lifts): Use the rotated killed Green gradient, source-free path integration, reflected stencils, and positive-order covariance tails. -> Local lifts, square-to-plane translation bounds, and bounded affine observation differences.
- **step-large-observation-bound** [claim](#stmt-prep-large-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-large-field): Insert deterministic face tilts, release common affine constraints, and combine the transform estimate with cutoff tails. -> Strict quadratic face saving for the cutoff interaction.
- **step-shape-localization** [claim](#stmt-prep-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-localization): Construct local trial extensions, isolate angular core energy, complete the massive observation square, and prove correction-source decay and graph comparison. -> Local shape stencil and exponentially localized correction matrix.
- **step-mesoscopic-entry** [claim](#stmt-prop-entry) / [proof](#sec:entry): Use exact tile isolation and localized numerical links, conditional restoration limits, strict real energy reserves, complex-strip interpolation, and connected-pattern summation. -> Small backgrounds, a polynomially bounded compulsory insertion, and exact copying.
- **step-uniform-map-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-lem-bulk-parity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bulk-parity): Bound Gaussian coordinate inverses uniformly, truncate future projection series, and replace the finite-depth kernels with their continuum limits. -> Common analytic bounds and quantitative absolute-index coefficient convergence.
- **step-periodic-diagnostics** [claim](#stmt-lem-bulk-physical-diagnostics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bulk-physical-diagnostics): Transfer rectangle limits through cuts and pins, then use replica parity independence and the limiting transform equation for phase factorization. -> Gaussian long-wave diagnostic and factorized mean-phase diagnostic on stopping tori.
- **step-no-exit** [claim](#stmt-lem-bulk-window-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bulk-window-vanishing): Use nonzero diagnostic multipliers at a candidate exit, then iterate remainder contraction and take interior-window limits. -> No exit and uniform vanishing on late finite-history windows.
- **step-positive-odd-sector** [claim](#stmt-lem-bulk-odd) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bulk-odd): Obtain a positive subexponential physical phase lower bound, force entrance into an invariant odd cone, and sum parity contractions. -> Positive odd coordinate and sharpened parity remainder bounds.
- **step-common-readouts** [claim](#stmt-prop-bulk-readouts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bulk-readouts): Compare each history and stopped torus with its exact Gaussian trajectory and telescope the integrated mean variances. -> History-independent microscopic readouts with nonlinear and exponential errors.
- **step-sparse-splice-lemma** [claim](#stmt-lem-trajectory-sparse-splice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trajectory-sparse-splice): Group each jump with preceding evolution, prove positive comparison cones, determine reciprocal-coordinate growth, and solve the ratio equation backward. -> Critical coordinate asymptotics with sparse jumps and correct ordinary-time counting.
- **step-overlap-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-trajectory-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trajectory-overlap): Equate the common physical readouts, establish charge comparability, invert the variance readout, and refine the phase comparison. -> Quadratic gradient and relative charge bounds between histories.
- **step-bulk-window-asymptotics** [claim](#stmt-prop-bulk-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bulk-window): Splice dyadic history segments at common indices, apply the sparse-jump lemma, compare unselected windows, and undo balance and Gaussian-curve subtraction. -> Unbalanced bulk gradient, charge, and remainder asymptotics on overlap windows.
- **step-common-magnetic-recursion** [claim](#stmt-prop-magnetic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-magnetic): Contract wall and defect backgrounds, back-translate marked scalar extraction, and extend exact partition algebra linearly in the insertion slot. -> Identical scalar ratios and insertion scalar, with a contracting residual insertion.
- **step-terminal-partition** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-partition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-partition): Integrate products with reserved terminal regulators, sum supports through anchor blocks, and bound partition degrees beyond the linear term. -> Positive terminal partition sums, first-order logarithmic expansion, and insertion bound.
- **step-relative-insertion-removal** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-scalar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-scalar): Compare the contracting residual and cutoff error with the crude magnetization lower bound, then infer scalar positivity from the exact physical identity. -> Logarithmic physical ratio with exponentially small relative errors.
- **step-first-order-double-difference** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-first-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-first-order): Separate bulk, wall, and near-mark supports; integrate gradient singletons, use evenness for wall translations, and compare only smooth anchored covariance tails near the mark. -> Running-gradient energy contribution with summably controlled terminal errors.
- **step-green-energy-increment** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-green) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-green): Use orthogonal dual energy minimization, telescope common finite-range Green pieces, and compare smoothed terminal sine sums with power accuracy. -> Deterministic logarithmic energy increment in the primary normalization.
- **step-summable-enlargement** [claim](#stmt-prop-ratio) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ratio): Subtract physical logarithmic identities, cancel common magnetic scalars, insert terminal energy and gradient estimates, and use the prescribed stopping ratio. -> Absolutely summable error in the geometric magnetization logarithmic increment.
- **step-positive-amplitude-and-all-sizes** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ratio-2): Sum compensated geometric logarithmic increments, then use sequential center ratios and subsequence compactness to pass to all integer sizes. -> Finite strictly positive amplitude and the full critical center-magnetization asymptotic.

          "anchor": "stmt-inp-partition-map",
          "exact_quote": "In the norms above, shell integration and designated local subtractions give an exact identity $$\\label{inp:exact-map}\n \\mathbb E\\mathcal Z_u(K;\\psi+\\zeta)\n   =\\prod_{b\\in\\mathcal B_{Lu}}(1+a_b)\n                                      \\mathcal Z_{Lu}(K';\\psi),\n \\qquad \\operatorname{Cov}(\\zeta)=\\Gamma_u.$$ The map is complex analytic in small activity inputs, uniformly in finite volume, and its nonlinear remainder is bounded by $C_{L,\\mathfrak A}\\varepsilon^2$ when the activity norm and separately measured singleton coefficients are at most $\\varepsilon$. It preserves the common-shift period and all symmetries respected by the localizations. The linear map restricted to large supports has arbitrarily small operator norm, relative to the input activity norm, after choosing $\\mathfrak A$ sufficiently large at fixed $L$.*\n\n*For small field-even neutral bulk inputs, subtraction of the constant and quadratic gradient term gives $CL^{-3}$ before counting fine placements. Subtracting only the constant gives $CL^{-2}$ for even inputs and $CL^{-1}$ without evenness. There are respectively $O(L^2)$, $O(L)$, and $O(1)$ placements at a bulk block, a specified wall, and a fixed mark. At $\\alpha^2=8\\pi$, the fundamental charge has gain $O(L^{-2})$; for field-even bulk inputs, subtraction of its constant-background cosine coefficient gives an additional $L^{-1}$. Higher charges have summable stronger gains.",

The coefficients are bounded above and away from zero, and $$\\label{inp:coefficient-limits}\n                         H_j\\to H>0,\\qquad b_j\\to2\\log L.$$ These are the map in Lemma 10.6 and the coefficient calculation in Proposition 8.5 of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)].",

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-bulk-physical-diagnostics",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bulk-physical-diagnostics</summary>\n\n*Proof.* We first transfer the height limit in (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 2.4) [[X](#ref-X)] from free rectangles to the periodic graph. For a smooth periodic function $v$, test the height by $(\\partial h,\\partial v_N)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:bulk-weighted-replica",
          "exact_quote": "For real $t$ define the transform and the difference-character $$F(t)=\\mathbb Ee^{i\\mu(h)+itX(h)},\\qquad\n \\Psi_t= e^{i\\mu(\\Delta)+itX(\\Delta)}.$$ The character $\\Psi_t$ depends only on the difference field and has modulus one. Conditional independence gives $\\mathbb E(A^2\\Psi_t)=\\mathbb E(V\\Psi_t)$. Inversion symmetry of the second replica gives $\\mathbb E\\Psi_t=F(t)^2$. The same sign reversal and differentiation of $F$ give, with primes denoting $t$ derivatives, $$\\mathbb E(A^2\\Psi_t)\n =(-F'')F+F(-F'')+2(-iF')(iF')\n =-2\\bigl(FF''-(F')^2\\bigr).$$ We have thus proved the exact finite-approximation identity $$\\label{eq:bulk-weighted-replica}\n -2\\bigl(FF''-(F')^2\\bigr)\n =2\\sigma^2F^2+\n   \\mathbb E\\bigl[(V-2\\sigma^2)\\Psi_t\\bigr],\n \\qquad t\\in\\mathbb R.$$ Cauchy–Schwarz and <a href=\"#eq:bulk-replica-variance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bulk-replica-variance\">[eq:bulk-replica-variance]</a> bound its last term by $\\sqrt{2\\kappa_4}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-bulk-physical-diagnostics"
    },

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-trajectory-sparse-splice",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-trajectory-sparse-splice</summary>\n\n*Proof.* We first obtain positive, comparable coordinates without assuming a rate of convergence. We then derive the rate $j^{-1}$, and finally identify its coefficient by solving the equation for $u/Z$ backwards.\n\n\n###### Grouping a jump with preceding evolution.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"

        {
          "anchor": "eq:trajectory-cones",
          "exact_quote": "Choose $c_+>0$ so large that $c_+^2\\beta_->2\\alpha_+$. Whenever $u\\ge c_+Z$, the same computation gives $$(u^+-c_+Z^+)-(u-c_+Z)\n   =-\\alpha Z^2+c_+\\beta uZ-c_+rZ\n   \\ge hZ^2$$ for some $h>0$ on a sufficiently late tail. This region is also forward invariant. Entry would make $u-c_+Z$ strictly positive and increasing, again contradicting convergence to zero. We have proved $$c_-Z<u<c_+Z\n \\label{eq:trajectory-cones}$$ at all sufficiently late retained nodes.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-magnetic",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:mag-differentiated-map",
          "exact_quote": "For clarity, we record the scalar algebra before estimating the insertion. Write one exact background map as $$\\label{eq:mag-one-map}\n \\mathbb E\\mathscr Z_k(K;\\phi+\\zeta)\n   =p_k(K)\\mathscr Z_{k+1}(\\mathcal F_k(K);\\phi),\n \\qquad\n p_k(K)=\\prod_b(1+a_{k,b}(K)).$$ Here the $a_{k,b}$ are the designated coefficients of small linear inputs. Extend the same exact splitting algebra to coefficients in $\\mathbb R[\\varepsilon]/(\\varepsilon^2)$. Its degree-zero terms use the background localization; its degree-one terms use the marked localization and allocation specified below. Write $$a_{k,b}(\\varepsilon)=a_{k,b}+\\varepsilon a'_{k,b},\n \\qquad K_+=\\mathcal F_k(K),\\qquad\n K_+(\\varepsilon)=K_++\\varepsilon\\mathcal T_k(K)I.$$ Thus $\\mathcal T_k(K)$ is the linear map in the insertion slot of this extended construction. It need not be the derivative of the unmarked map $\\mathcal F_k$, since the two slots have different localization prescriptions. Put $$I_+=\\mathcal T_k(K)I,\n \\qquad\n \\beta_k=\\sum_b\\frac{a'_{k,b}}{1+a_{k,b}}.$$ The coefficient of $\\varepsilon$ in the exact identity is $$\\label{eq:mag-differentiated-map}\n \\mathbb E\\mathscr I_k(K,I;\\phi+\\zeta)\n =p_k(K)\\left\\{\\beta_k\\mathscr Z_{k+1}(K_+;\\phi)\n                    +\\mathscr I_{k+1}(K_+,I_+;\\phi)\\right\\}.$$ This is differentiation of the partition identity with the stated degree-dependent splittings. No finite nonzero perturbation by the possibly large $I$ is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:mag-scalar-recursion",
          "exact_quote": "If an earlier step has already produced a scalar $\\sigma$, linearity applied to $\\sigma\\mathscr Z_k(K)+\\mathscr I_k(K,I)$ gives $$\\label{eq:mag-scalar-recursion}\n \\sigma_+=\\sigma+\\beta_k,\n \\qquad I_+=\\mathcal T_k(K)I.$$ In particular the earlier scalar is kept outside the later map. It is not put back into the activity as $\\sigma K$. This elementary identity is important because $\\sigma$ need not be small, whereas the operator controlling $I_+$ only sees the small background $K$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-magnetic",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prop-magnetic</summary>\n\n*Proof of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:magnetic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:magnetic\">22</a>.* The constructions above give the exact identities and the copying assertions. We complete the uniform estimates and the parameter choices.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-terminal-partition",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-terminal-partition</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* The terminal regulator estimate in Proposition <a href=\"#inp:regulator\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"inp:regulator\">6</a> gives $$\\mathbb E_{*,Q}\\prod_{a=1}^p\\mathcal G_h(X_a;\\psi)\n \\leq C^{|X_1|+\\cdots+|X_p|}\n \\label{eq:terminal-regulator}$$ for compatible supports, with any one of the fixed reserved regulators $\\mathcal G_h$ used here.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-terminal-scalar",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-terminal-scalar</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* The bounds on $s$, the entry index $i$, and the terminal index $h$ give $s=O_L(j^3)$, $i=O_L(\\log j)$, and $j-h=O_L(\\log j)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:terminal-exact-ratio",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\widehat a_{Q/2}$ denote the ratio with the observation cutoffs used for this history. This notation depends on the chosen history; only the two squares under simultaneous comparison use it below. Set $$E_Q=\\|\\eta_{l,Q}\\|^2,\n \\qquad H_Q=\\exp\\{-E_Q/(2\\alpha^2)\\}.$$ The common preparation scalar cancels between the two sectors. The exact identities <a href=\"#eq:mag-background-products\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mag-background-products\">[eq:mag-background-products]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:mag-insertion-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mag-insertion-identity\">[eq:mag-insertion-identity]</a> therefore give $$\\widehat a_{Q/2}\n =H_Q\\rho_j\n       \\frac{\\sigma_j\\mathcal Z_Q^l+\\mathcal J_Q}\n            {\\mathcal Z_Q^0},\n \\qquad\n \\rho_j=\\frac{P_Q^l}{P_Q^0}>0.\n \\label{eq:terminal-exact-ratio}$$ Both $\\rho_j$ and $\\sigma_j$ are exactly the same for $Q=M$ and $Q=LM$. The equality of $\\sigma_j$ is algebraic; its sign has not been assumed.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-first-order",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-terminal-first-order",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-terminal-first-order</summary>\n\n*Proof.* We split the supports into shift-good bulk supports, shift-good wall supports, and the remaining supports near the mark. These are the same support classifications used for the copying prescriptions in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:magnetic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:magnetic\">22</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:terminal-tail",
          "exact_quote": "We can consequently realize the restrictions of the two fields as a common Gaussian part plus respective independent tails. Anchor each tail at a fixed point in the enlarged rectangle. The positive-order terminal covariance estimates at scale $c_3M$ imply, for every fixed $r\\geq2$, $$\\bigl\\|\\text{anchored tail}\\bigr\\|_{L^r(\\text{scaled test norm})}\n       \\leq \\frac{C_{L,r}\\operatorname{poly}(p)}D.\n \\label{eq:terminal-tail}$$ For completeness, the difference estimates through order five bound the scaled differences of orders one and two in supremum on each $v$-block by the rescaled discrete Sobolev estimate used in the regulator proof. Summing the first differences along paths from the anchor bounds the field value itself. The enclosing rectangle has block dimensions $O_L(1+p)$, so these sums introduce only a polynomial factor in $p$. Gaussian moment estimates give the stated $L^r$ bounds. The factor $v/M=D^{-1}$ comes from the first positive-order tail estimate.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-first-order-double-difference",
      "inputs": [
        "Bulk remainder control",
        "Exact ordinary and pierced copying",
        "Positive-order lift and covariance estimates"
      ],
      "method": "Separate bulk, wall, and near-mark supports; integrate gradient singletons, use evenness for wall translations, and compare only smooth anchored covariance tails near the mark.",
      "output": "Running-gradient energy contribution with summably controlled terminal errors.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-terminal-first-order"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-green",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-fixed-boundary-duality",
        "step-local-lifts"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:terminal-green-energy",
          "exact_quote": "The orthogonal energy minimization and crossing correspondence in Equation <a href=\"#inp:connection-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inp:connection-energy\">[inp:connection-energy]</a> give the exact identity $$E_M=(2\\pi)^2G_n(0,0).\n \\label{eq:terminal-green-energy}$$ We verify the accuracy needed for the Green increment: $$G_{Ln}(0,0)-G_n(0,0)\n       =\\frac{\\log L}{2\\pi}+O_L(n^{-c_4}).\n \\label{eq:terminal-green-increment}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:terminal-green-telescope",
          "exact_quote": "$$G_{Ln}(0,0)-G_n(0,0)\n   =\\sum_{r=0}^{\\log_2L-1}d_{2^r\\rho M}+T_{LM}-T_M.\n \\label{eq:terminal-green-telescope}$$ The plane diagonal estimate for each of these $\\log_2L$ terms is $\\log2/(2\\pi)$ with an error bounded by a negative power of its scale. This is the continuum kernel estimate in (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 8.1) [[B](#ref-B)]; the value of the limiting diagonal is the radial integral $$\\frac1{2\\pi}\\int_0^\\infty\n      \\frac{P(r)-P(2r)}r\\,dr=\\frac{\\log2}{2\\pi},$$ where $P$ is the continuum low-pass multiplier, $P(0)=1$, and $P(r)\\to0$ at infinity. Since their scales are comparable to $n$, their total contribution is $\\log L/(2\\pi)+O_L(n^{-c})$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        },

          "anchor": "eq:terminal-sine-sum",
          "exact_quote": "The two smoothed terminal diagonals have the same continuum limit, with power accuracy. We verify this stronger quantitative assertion directly. The eigenvalues of the killed square are $$\\lambda_{k,M}\n   =4\\sin^2\\frac{\\pi k_1}{2M}\n       +4\\sin^2\\frac{\\pi k_2}{2M},\n \\qquad 1\\leq k_1,k_2\\leq M-1.$$ The smoothed terminal diagonal at the center is consequently $$T_M=\\frac4{M^2}\n     \\sum_{k_1,k_2=1}^{M-1}\n     \\sin^2\\frac{\\pi k_1}{2}\\sin^2\\frac{\\pi k_2}{2}\n     \\frac{q_{\\rho M}(\\lambda_{k,M})}{\\lambda_{k,M}}.\n \\label{eq:terminal-sine-sum}$$ The elementary eigenvalue bounds and the multiplier estimate give $$M^2\\lambda_{k,M}\\geq c|k|^2,\n \\qquad\n |q_{\\rho M}(\\lambda_{k,M})|\n       \\leq C_\\rho\\min\\{1,|k|^{-2p}\\},$$ where $p$ is the fixed power in the kernel construction. Thus the part of <a href=\"#eq:terminal-sine-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:terminal-sine-sum\">[eq:terminal-sine-sum]</a> with $|k|>B$ is bounded by $C_\\rho B^{-c}$ for some $c>0$, uniformly in $M$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-critical-center-observable**: The asymptotic concerns center magnetization in the planar nearest-neighbor XY square with aligned boundary spins, evaluated at the mass-defined critical threshold and conditional on the stated companion inputs.
- **invariant-exact-dual-boundary-geometry**: The positive center observable equals the shifted-to-centered dual height partition ratio with a circulation at the center. The retained dual edges include those crossing bonds incident to the fixed spin boundary.
- **invariant-sparse-splice-ordinary-time**: The marginal trajectory estimates survive geometrically separated changes of history when ordinary evolution and jumps satisfy their distinct error bounds. A history change does not add an ordinary evolution step.
- **invariant-common-magnetic-scalars**: The pierced-to-ordinary extraction ratio and the extracted insertion scalar agree exactly between the compared squares. Residual insertions retain the mark, and the extraction products exclude the preparation scalar and harmonic-energy factor.
- **invariant-relative-insertion-removal**: The residual insertion is removed relative to the positive physical observable. Positivity of the extracted insertion scalar follows from the exact representation and relative error control.
- **invariant-summable-normalization**: The compensated logarithm has absolutely summable increments along geometric sizes. Sequential center comparison then transfers its finite limit to all integer sizes, yielding a positive finite amplitude.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-unpinned-center-magnetization**: Apply the center-magnetization asymptotic to a free or periodic spin square. -> The observable and boundary law differ from those in the theorem; periodic height diagnostics do not establish the same spin observable under another boundary law.
- **anti-use-ratio-limit-as-logarithmic-normalization**: Infer the logarithmic correction and amplitude from fixed-ratio center comparison alone. -> A slowly varying multiplier remains compatible with the center comparison input. The summable enlargement estimate supplies the additional normalization information.
- **anti-use-finite-history-as-unrestricted-flow**: Continue a prepared cutoff history indefinitely or impose a convergence rate on its entry data. -> The cutoff is useful only over a finite volume range, and the proof compares overlapping histories without a rate in the height scaling limit.
- **anti-use-uncontrolled-or-dense-history-jumps**: Apply the sparse-splice asymptotics to arbitrary history resets. -> The backward ratio estimate and accumulated inverse-coordinate errors rely on geometric jump separation and the stated coordinate-dependent jump bounds.
- **anti-use-hidden-numerical-dependence**: Declare an activity local because its explicit field arguments are near the source. -> A coefficient may still depend on a distant wall through its numerical calculation, invalidating exact copying and common scalar extraction.
- **anti-use-reinsert-extracted-scalar**: Put the accumulated insertion scalar back into the background activity before the next map. -> The scalar need not be small; reinserting it changes the decomposition used to control the residual insertion operator.
- **anti-use-absolute-residual-error**: Discard the residual insertion merely because its absolute size tends to zero. -> Center magnetization also tends to zero, so an absolute error bound alone does not justify a logarithm or scalar positivity.
- **anti-use-finite-check-as-amplitude-certificate**: Treat numerical agreement at finitely many sizes as certification of the critical amplitude. -> The theorem concludes an infinite-size limit from companion inputs, summable errors, and sequential comparison; finite observations do not prove those preconditions or determine a certified amplitude.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal result is a conditional infinite-size magnetization asymptotic, rather than a finite constructive input-output claim. Its amplitude is obtained from convergence of the compensated logarithm, not from an effective finite approximation procedure with a certified stopping criterion. The concluding proof states: "The increments are absolutely summable." It then states: "The finiteness of $B_\infty$ gives both $A_{\rm XY}>0$ and $A_{\rm XY}<\infty$." These passages occur at the concluding theorem proof anchor. Finite Green-function calculations or truncated recurrences would test auxiliary instances without deciding the required infinite trajectory, companion scaling limits, or amplitude convergence.

For every integer $j\\ge j_0$, let $(u_j,Z_j)$ and $(\\widetilde u_{j+1},\\widetilde Z_{j+1})$ be real states with positive second coordinates. Assume that both coordinates of every state tend to zero as its scale index tends to infinity. Then, with $x_*=(H/b)^{1/2}$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac{u_j}{Z_j}&=x_*+O(j^{-1}),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-ratio}\\\\\n \\frac1{Z_j}&=b x_*j+O(\\log j),\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-inverse}\\\\\n u_j&=\\frac1{bj}+O\\left(\\frac{\\log j}{j^2}\\right),\\qquad\n Z_j=\\frac1{\\sqrt{bH}\\,j}\n                 +O\\left(\\frac{\\log j}{j^2}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:trajectory-asymptotics}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same estimates hold for the states immediately before jumps.",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The center-magnetization asymptotic supplies a mathematical benchmark, but does not specify an estimator, finite-size error tolerance, or software implementation contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The observable belongs to an equilibrium spin law with aligned boundary vertices. The paper supplies no time evolution or simulation procedure for that law. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The analytic entry construction requires compatible geometry and regulator reserves chosen through limiting arguments. It does not provide an executable parameter-selection contract. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sparse-splice result determines an infinite trajectory from convergence assumptions and uniformly controlled recurrences. A finite prefix cannot certify those tail assumptions. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polymer representation includes compatibility exclusions and numerical dependence collars. Although these describe mathematical organization, the paper gives no storage or update contract for a data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Uniform activity-norm contraction concerns Gaussian shell integration and support localization. It establishes no hardware execution model, throughput bound, or resource guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The characteristic-coefficient comparisons concern height laws under added precision. They do not construct a security primitive or establish a computational hardness assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The physical readouts recover marginal coordinates of the specified height recursion. No learning objective, training procedure, or prediction guarantee is constructed. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | For a finite preparation graph, the affine shape energy defines a concrete quadratic minimization over site fields. The proof constructs the minimizer by correcting a local trial field with the inverse of the positive observation-energy operator. This supports a finite energy-solving component within the paper's geometry. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Exact copying between finite histories depends on covariance range, geometric clearance, and recorded numerical dependence. It supplies no communication, consistency, or fault-recovery construction for distributed processes. |
| other (Discrete potential theory) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite killed-Laplacian Green function gives a concrete construction of the minimum circulation energy in a dual square. Solving the finite Dirichlet problem provides that energy; the enlargement asymptotic remains an analytical result rather than a finite numerical certificate. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-companion-input-dependence** (hypothesis): The main magnetization asymptotic is conditional on the stated height, local renormalization, and spin-field inputs. The present argument does not independently replace those companion results. Blocked use: ['Presenting the asymptotic as an unconditional conclusion independent of the companion inputs.']
- **limit-aligned-boundary-observable** (hypothesis): The observable is the center cosine expectation with every boundary spin aligned and every prescribed interior bond retained, including bonds incident to the boundary. Blocked use: ['Applying the center-magnetization normalization to free or periodic spin boundary conditions.', 'Deleting boundary-incident bonds while retaining the stated duality.']
- **limit-primary-planar-model** (formal_scope): The proof concerns the planar nearest-neighbor cosine model and its dual square-face height graph. Extension to other dimensions, spin spaces, interaction laws, or domain shapes is not established here. Blocked use: ['Transferring the exponents to a different spin model without proving the corresponding duality and critical inputs.', 'Using the square result as a theorem for arbitrary geometry.']
- **limit-mass-defined-critical-threshold** (hypothesis): The magnetization is evaluated at the mass-defined critical threshold. The paper does not establish its logarithmic normalization for an arbitrary inverse temperature. Blocked use: ['Applying the critical formula away from the prescribed threshold.', 'Replacing the threshold by a numerical estimate without accounting for its uncertainty.']
- **limit-unit-edge-normalization** (formal_scope): The connection-energy calculation uses the unscaled unit-edge Laplacian killed on the spin-square boundary. Changing the Laplacian normalization requires changing the corresponding energy coefficients. Blocked use: ['Substituting a differently normalized Laplacian while preserving the stated energy increment.', 'Counting an undirected bond repeatedly in the primary energy.']
- **limit-affine-comparison-and-tilt** (edge_case): The shifted-to-centered upper bound applies to the specified un-tilted partition ratio. The proposition explicitly allows a tilted ratio to exceed that bound, and its normalized exponential-moment assertion excludes a zero-cost denominator. Blocked use: ['Bounding every real-tilted ratio by the un-tilted upper bound.', 'Dividing a statistic by the square root of a vanishing reference cost.']
- **limit-mesh-and-geometry-order** (hypothesis): The height and pin inputs use fixed nondegenerate geometries with positive-width passages and separated pin regions. Mesh refinement precedes subsequent variation of the fixed geometry. Blocked use: ['Assuming uniform limits for degenerating passages or colliding pin regions.', 'Interchanging geometry variation with mesh refinement without additional estimates.']
- **limit-cutoff-finite-volume-error** (approximation): The cutoff error is centered-normalized and contains a volume-dependent factor. In a magnetic sector it must be compared with the positive center observable before it becomes a relative error. Blocked use: ['Treating the cutoff error as uniform over unrestricted volumes at fixed observation scale.', 'Taking logarithms after establishing only an absolute magnetic error bound.']
- **limit-lift-clearance-and-positive-derivatives** (hypothesis): Local gauging requires the source to lie outside the enlarged rectangle. The square-to-plane derivative estimates are restricted to an inner fixed fraction of the square and do not assert a uniform bound on an absolute branch potential. Blocked use: ['Gauging away circulation on a patch containing the source.', 'Extending the interior lift estimate to arbitrary wall regions or absolute field values.']
- **limit-local-circulation-energy** (edge_case): Affine shape localization retains a local circulation contribution when the pierced star is present. Exponential decay of the correction matrix does not remove that local core energy. Blocked use: ['Discarding the logarithmic circulation term when bounding the affine shape energy.', 'Assigning the core-energy cost to the exponentially small nonlocal links.']
- **limit-entry-reserves-and-ordered-choices** (hypothesis): Mesoscopic entry requires compatible map parameters, entry-admissible regulator reserves, and sufficiently refined compatible observation cells. Ordinary background smallness does not imply that the compulsory insertion is small. Blocked use: ['Choosing entry parameters independently of the downstream norm and geometric reserves.', 'Applying a small-background perturbation argument to the entire initial insertion.']
- **limit-qualitative-entry-refinement** (nonconstructive): The compact-data transfer uses qualitative refinement convergence after geometry and tolerance are fixed. An effective uniform refinement threshold is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Deriving executable default entry settings from the existential parameter choices.', 'Claiming a certified finite stopping criterion for the entry transfer.']
- **limit-complete-numerical-dependence** (hypothesis): Exact copying requires clearance for the full calculation, including numerical dependence boxes, support paths, collars, and any wall or winding dependence. Nearby field arguments alone do not establish locality. Blocked use: ['Identifying coefficients between geometries while ignoring a distant dependence boundary.', 'Removing wall or winding labels before applying exact copying.']
- **limit-finite-history-horizon** (formal_scope): Each prepared cutoff history has a finite horizon. The bulk estimates compare coordinates at common absolute indices and establish uniform conclusions only on the stated windows. Blocked use: ['Continuing a single cutoff history indefinitely using only its finite-horizon estimates.', 'Comparing histories by elapsed steps since entry rather than common physical scale.']
- **limit-sparse-splice-tail-assumptions** (hypothesis): The sparse-splice asymptotics require positive second coordinates, convergence of every state to zero, the stated ordinary recurrences, uniform error bounds, and geometrically separated changes of history. A history change is not an additional ordinary step. Blocked use: ['Applying the lemma to arbitrary dense resets or uncontrolled jump errors.', 'Certifying the infinite-tail assumptions from a finite recurrence prefix.', 'Counting history replacements as additional evolution time.']
- **limit-common-stop-and-linear-insertion** (hypothesis): Common magnetic scalar cancellation depends on the same finite history, common stop, exact marked copying, and the original compatibility exclusions. The extracted scalar remains outside later activity maps. Blocked use: ['Cancelling scalars obtained from unrelated histories or stopping scales.', 'Reinserting the accumulated scalar into the background activity.', 'Replacing the compulsory insertion functional by an unrestricted product.']
- **limit-terminal-error-balance** (approximation): Terminal integration uses small backgrounds, reserved regulators, and the prescribed relation between stopping scale and square size. Wall translations require evenness, while near-mark comparisons use smooth anchored covariance tails. Blocked use: ['Choosing an arbitrary terminal block ratio while preserving the claimed summable error.', 'Estimating wall contributions only by their absolute activity norm.', 'Comparing microscopic Gaussian densities in place of the controlled tail argument.']
- **limit-relative-residual-and-scalar-sign** (hypothesis): Positivity of the extracted insertion scalar is established only after residual and cutoff errors are negligible relative to the positive physical magnetization. It is not an initial positivity assumption on individual activities. Blocked use: ['Inferring scalar positivity directly from the algebraic extraction.', 'Discarding a residual because its absolute size vanishes without comparing it to magnetization.']
- **limit-ratio-versus-normalization** (formal_scope): Sequential center ratios permit an arbitrary slowly varying factor. The logarithmic multiplier and finite amplitude require the additional summable enlargement estimate. Blocked use: ['Deriving the complete normalization from fixed-ratio scaling alone.', 'Replacing absolute summability by qualitative convergence of enlargement ratios.']
- **limit-amplitude-effectivity-and-finite-certification** (nonconstructive): The model-specific amplitude is obtained from a limiting compensated logarithm. A numerical amplitude, effective finite-size acceptance tolerance, and finite certificate for the complete asymptotic are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Using the asymptotic as an exact finite-size equality.', 'Treating agreement at finitely many sizes as certification of the amplitude or companion limits.', 'Transferring the amplitude unchanged to another model.']

          "exact_quote": "On each open preparation graph there are a quadratic form $L_G$ with a fixed-radius stencil in the faces, and a symmetric matrix $N_G$ on those faces, such that $$\\label{prep:shape-decomposition}\n E_G^l(y)=L_G(d)+\\sum_{f,g\\in\\mathcal F(G)}N_G(f,g)d_fd_g,$$ where $$\\label{prep:trial-energy}\n 0\\le L_G(d)\\le C\\sum_fd_f^2\n                       +C\\log(2s)\\sum_z\\kappa_z(d)^2.$$ The forms and matrices may be defined on arbitrary face arrays $d$, without imposing a curl constraint. For the actual one-source data this implies $$\\label{eq:affine-shape-bound}\n E_G^l(y)\\le C\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F(G)}d_f^2\n                  +C\\log(2s)\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\text{pierced star present}\\}}.$$",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-entry",
          "exact_quote": "Choose the regulator reserves as in <a href=\"#eq:entry-admissible-reserve\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:entry-admissible-reserve\">[eq:entry-admissible-reserve]</a>, and fix compatible parameters for the local maps of (OpenAI 2026a) [[B](#ref-B)], including the block factor $L$, the support geometry, the activity weight $\\mathfrak A$, and the analytic radius. A fixed finite collection of stronger weights and entry-admissible regulator regimes may be specified. For every $\\epsilon>0$, one can choose a fixed dyadic $q$, an arbitrarily large dyadic $R$, and then $s_0$, such that the following conclusions hold for every compatible dyadic $s\\ge s_0$, with $P=R^5$ and $m=qRs$.*\n\n\n1.  *In the ordinary case, averaging the observation noise and the reference covariance below $m$ gives local activities $K_m$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:entry-small}\n                     \\lVert K_m\\rVert_{m,\\mathfrak A,V}\\le\\epsilon.$$ This holds on finite plane supports, on compatible tori, and on free squares whose sides are divisible by $sP$ and are at least $sP$. Up to a positive field-independent scalar, the corresponding subset partition function is exactly the averaged cutoff interaction. Activities with their required clearance from a wall or a torus identification are the corresponding plane activities. The construction is real, even, and covariant under the block translations and square symmetries that preserve the chosen hierarchy.*\n\n\n\n2.  *In a pierced free square in which the compulsory footprint and its required collars fit, the same scalar as in its ordinary counterpart may be extracted. The remaining partition function is $$\\label{eq:entry-linear-partition}\n                        \\mathcal Z_m^{(1)}(K_m^l,I_m;\\psi),$$ with exactly one insertion activity $I_m$. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Assuming the stated critical-height, local-renormalization, and spin-field inputs from the companion papers, we determine the center magnetization of the planar XY model in a square with aligned boundary spins at its mass-defined critical threshold. As $`n\to\infty`$, the magnetization is $`A_{\mathrm{XY}}n^{-1/8}(\log n)^{1/16}(1+o(1))`$, where $`A_{\mathrm{XY}}`$ is a finite, strictly positive model-specific constant.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For an integer $R\geq1$, let $\Lambda_R=[-R,R]^2\cap\mathbb Z^2$. The planar XY model assigns an angle $\theta_x\in\mathbb R/(2\pi\mathbb Z)$ to each vertex. With free boundary conditions and inverse temperature $b>0$, its probability density is proportional to $$\exp\left\{b\sum_{\substack{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda_R\\|x-y|=1}}
                      \cos(\theta_x-\theta_y)\right\},$$ relative to product uniform measure. Each undirected edge is counted once, and there are no exterior bonds. Write $\mathbb E^{\mathrm{free}}_{R,b}$ for expectation under this law, and define <a id="eq:intro-threshold"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-threshold}
 C_b(r)=\lim_{R\to\infty}\mathbb E^{\mathrm{free}}_{R,b}
       \cos(\theta_0-\theta_{re_1}),\qquad
 m(b)=\lim_{r\to\infty}-\frac{1}{r}\log C_b(r),\qquad
 b_c=\inf\{b>0:m(b)=0\}.$$ The existence of these limits and the fact that $0<b_c<\infty$ with $m(b_c)=0$ are among the inputs recalled in Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a>.

In $\Lambda_n$, now fix $\theta_x=0$ at every boundary vertex and retain every nearest-neighbor bond inside $\Lambda_n$, including those incident to its boundary. Let $\mathbb E^0_{n,b}$ denote this law. Our observable is $$a_n=\mathbb E^0_{n,b_c}\cos\theta_0.$$ It measures how strongly an aligned boundary influences the center at criticality. The power-law exponent alone does not determine its normalization: a slowly varying multiplier can remain invisible to fixed-ratio scaling limits.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Using the height, renormalization, and spin-field inputs from (OpenAI 2026a, 2026b, 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[B](#ref-B), [X](#ref-X), [S](#ref-S)] stated in Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a>, there is a constant $A_{\mathrm{XY}}\in(0,\infty)$ such that $$\label{eq:main}
 a_n=A_{\mathrm{XY}}\,n^{-1/8}(\log n)^{1/16}(1+o(1))
                       \qquad(n\to\infty).$$*

</div>

The dependence on the three companion results is substantive. The height limit fixes the critical Gaussian coefficient, the local maps supply the analytic renormalization framework, and the spin-field results provide a rough positive lower bound and a ratio limit. We state the portions used below before beginning the proof. In particular, the critical magnetic contraction and the exact cancellation of its scalar factors are proved here; they are not asserted as consequences of a low-temperature magnetic-flow theorem.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Historical context

The critical behavior of two-dimensional systems with continuous symmetry differs from the conventional picture of ordering through spontaneous magnetization. Berezinskii’s analysis of the spin-wave regime and the vortex picture developed by Kosterlitz and Thouless identified the mechanism behind a phase with algebraic correlations (Berezinskii 1971; Kosterlitz and Thouless 1973) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Berezinskii1971](#ref-Berezinskii1971), [KosterlitzThouless1973](#ref-KosterlitzThouless1973)]. Kosterlitz’s renormalization analysis predicted the critical exponent and the essential singularity of the correlation length (Kosterlitz 1974) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Kosterlitz1974](#ref-Kosterlitz1974)]. The treatment of vortices and symmetry-breaking perturbations by José, Kadanoff, Kirkpatrick, and Nelson made the relation with Coulomb-gas and periodic-field descriptions particularly explicit (José et al. 1977) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[JoseEtAl1977](#ref-JoseEtAl1977)]. These works supply the physical and methodological ancestry of the marginal recursion used here.

Rigorous correlation estimates established important parts of this picture without determining the exact critical normalization. McBryan and Spencer’s complex-rotation argument gives an algebraic upper bound (McBryan and Spencer 1977) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[McBryanSpencer1977](#ref-McBryanSpencer1977)]. Fröhlich and Spencer proved low-temperature algebraic lower bounds for the plane rotator and related models (Fröhlich and Spencer 1981, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[FrohlichSpencer1981](#ref-FrohlichSpencer1981)]. More recently, van Engelenburg and Lis proved the exponential–polynomial dichotomy with a polynomial lower bound also at the critical point (Engelenburg and Lis 2023, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[VanEngelenburgLis2023](#ref-VanEngelenburgLis2023)]. Lammers identified the correlation-length relation between the XY model and its dual height model, relating the massless and delocalized phases (Lammers 2023, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Lammers2023](#ref-Lammers2023)]. These results concern the transition and quantitative correlation bounds; they do not determine the logarithmic factor in <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a>.

The relevance of logarithmic corrections has long been recognized in finite-size scaling. For example, Kenna and Irving discuss the renormalization-group prediction $\chi_N\asymp N^{7/4}(\log N)^{1/8}$ for the susceptibility on a periodic square (Kenna and Irving 1995) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KennaIrving1995](#ref-KennaIrving1995)]. This is a prediction for a different observable, not a proof of the aligned-boundary one-point asymptotic. A square-root relation for the root-mean-square spatially averaged magnetization cannot by itself identify the center observable studied here.

Rigorous renormalization methods also give more precise information in related models. Dimock and Hurd control the ultraviolet-regularized sine-Gordon model at weak coupling with parameter $\beta>8\pi$ (Dimock and Hurd 2000) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DimockHurd2000](#ref-DimockHurd2000)]. Falco constructs marginal trajectories of order $1/j$ for the dilute Coulomb gas and proves multiplicative logarithms for suitable fractional-charge correlations (Falco 2012, 2013) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Falco2012](#ref-Falco2012), [Falco2013](#ref-Falco2013)]. Those probe observables and their logarithmic exponents are not interchangeable with magnetic observables of the nearest-neighbor cosine model. The common mathematical feature is that a coupling which decays as $1/j$, when accumulated over scales, produces a power of a logarithm.

Our immediate inputs are the three companion manuscripts (OpenAI 2026a, 2026b, 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[B](#ref-B), [X](#ref-X), [S](#ref-S)]. Their roles are different. The local maps and analytic norms come from (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[B](#ref-B)]; the critical Bessel-height coefficient $8\pi$ and the required mixed-geometry height limits come from (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[X](#ref-X)]. The spin-field construction in (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[S](#ref-S)] uses the exact center magnetization as its normalization and leaves its asymptotic size unspecified. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> identifies that size. The additional work is to enter the critical maps on finite histories, compare those histories without a rate in the height limit, and cancel the magnetic scalar factors exactly before estimating terminal errors. We do not use the public results for related models as substitutes for these companion inputs.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

The logarithmic factor in <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a> reflects the marginal coordinate of the critical height recursion. The positive finite amplitude requires the finer, summable control described next.

Fourier duality expresses $a_n$ as a ratio of two partition sums for heights in $2\pi\mathbb Z$. The numerator has a circulation of $2\pi$ around the center, whereas the denominator has zero circulation. The proof has two principal difficulties. First, the observation density of the discrete height model cannot simply be assumed small in a Gaussian large-field norm. Second, entering the recursion in the presence of a circulation defect can create a large normalization factor. An estimate of that factor would be an unnecessary additional problem.

We resolve the first difficulty by working on overlapping finite intervals of scales. At a large target index $J$, introduce observations on cells of side comparable to $J^3$, and remove exceptionally large neighboring observation differences by a smooth cutoff. The cutoff error is exponentially small in $J^3$, whereas the finite physical boxes used in this history have logarithmic side of order $J$. The resulting interaction is small in the full analytic norm of the height maps. This construction is carried out in Sections <a href="#sec:preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preparation">3</a> and <a href="#sec:entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:entry">4</a>.

<a id="eq:intro-running"></a>
Each history then runs far enough beyond its target interval to determine the critical branch of the recursion. Two observables of the original height law, the variance of a long-wave fluctuation and a characteristic coefficient of the mean height, compare the coordinates of different histories at a common absolute scale. Section <a href="#sec:bulk" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bulk">5</a> proves these comparisons without assuming a rate for the height scaling limit. Section <a href="#sec:trajectory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:trajectory">6</a> combines them with the marginal recursion. Its conclusion for the running gradient coefficient $t_j$, at block side $L^j$, is $$\label{eq:intro-running}
 t_j=\frac{1}{2j\log L}
                 +O\!\left(\frac{\log j}{j^2}\right),$$ where $L$ is a fixed, sufficiently large power of two. Sparse changes of history have summable effects on this asymptotic.

To resolve the second difficulty, compare squares of side $2n$ and $2Ln$ in the dual height representation using the same history and the same stopping scale. Section <a href="#sec:magnetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:magnetic">7</a> keeps the circulation insertion linear throughout the exact partition algebra. All scalar factors produced near the insertion are identical in the two squares. Their size is immaterial because they cancel in the ratio. The residual insertion contracts quickly enough that the rough lower bound on $a_n$ makes its contribution negligible even relatively.

What remains is a terminal Gaussian calculation. The energy of the circulation produces the leading power $n^{-1/8}$; its coupling to <a href="#eq:intro-running" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-running">[eq:intro-running]</a> produces the logarithmic correction. Section <a href="#sec:terminal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:terminal">8</a> proves, for $n=L^j/2$, <a id="eq:intro-ratio"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-ratio}
 \log a_{Ln}-\log a_n
  =-\frac{\log L}{8}+\frac{1}{16j}
                  +O(j^{-1-\epsilon})$$ for some $\epsilon>0$. Absolute summability, rather than an estimate of the initial magnetic normalization, yields the finite positive amplitude. The spin-field ratio limit then transfers the result from these geometric sizes to all integers.

<a id="sec:inputs"></a>
### Height conventions and local map inputs

The proof combines three inputs: the critical height coefficient from (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[X](#ref-X)], the local integration maps of (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[B](#ref-B)], and the identical-nest comparison of (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[S](#ref-S)]. We first fix their common normalization. We then specify the analytic spaces in which the local maps operate. These inputs include finite-graph inequalities, scaling limits, and local activity maps. The entry construction for the critical XY model is supplied below.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Dual heights and finite comparisons

For a graph $G$ consisting of retained nearest-neighbor edges, choose one orientation of each edge and write $\partial v$ for the oriented gradient. Its unit-conductance Laplacian is $A_G=\partial^*\partial$, so that <a id="inp:energy-convention"></a>$$\label{inp:energy-convention}
 \langle v,A_Gv\rangle=\sum_{\{x,y\}\in E(G)}(v_y-v_x)^2.$$ Thus each undirected edge contributes once. Heights take values in $2\pi\mathbb Z$, and an edge difference $2\pi k$ has weight $$p_b(k)=e^{-b}I_k(b),\qquad
 \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}p_b(k)e^{tk}=\exp\{b(\cosh t-1)\}.$$ An integral connection $l$ is an edge cochain with values in $2\pi\mathbb Z$; it replaces $\partial h$ by $\partial h+l$ in the weights. Write $Z_G^l$ for the resulting sum and $F_G^l=Z_G^l/Z_G^0$. Without observations or absolute pins, a constant translation by $2\pi\mathbb Z$ is divided out on each free component. If observations confine the constants, their translation copies are summed instead. Changing $l$ by an integral gradient is an exact change of height variables.

<a id="stmt-inp-duality"></a>
<div id="inp:duality" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Fixed-boundary duality). *Work at $b=b_c$. Let $G_M$ be the free graph of the $M^2$ unit square faces in $[-n,n]^2$, where $M=2n$. If $l$ has circulation $2\pi$ around the central primal vertex and zero circulation around all other interior primal vertices, then $$a_n=F_{G_{2n}}^l>0.$$ The graph retains the edges crossing primal bonds incident to the fixed spin boundary.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-inp-duality"><summary>Proof of stmt-inp-duality</summary>

*Proof.* This is the fixed-spin duality of (OpenAI 2026c, Lemma 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[S](#ref-S)]. In its Fourier current expansion, integration imposes current divergence only at interior spin vertices. Bonds lying along the boundary have both spins fixed and cancel from the ratio. Every other retained primal bond crosses one dual bond, including a bond incident to the boundary. Subtract a unit current from the center to the boundary and rotate the remaining divergence-free current. It is a height gradient, uniquely modulo one common translation, and the subtracted current gives $l$. The factors $e^b$ cancel. All remaining weights are positive. ◻

</details>

We shall use the following finite-graph rules in exactly this normalization. A centered precision means an additional nonnegative quadratic form, including a limit enforcing centered linear equalities. A reference cost for a linear statistic $X=\sum_e w_e\partial_eh$ is $\mathcal R(w)=\sum_e w_e^2$.

<a id="stmt-inp-comparisons"></a>
<div id="inp:comparisons" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Finite comparison inputs). *The comparisons in (OpenAI 2026b, Lemmas 2.2 and 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[X](#ref-X)] hold on every fixed primary graph, also with independent Gaussian observation noises.*

1.  *Adding centered precision decreases centered covariance and centered real moment generating functions. Covariance in an affine sector, including after a real tilt, dominates centered covariance. In particular, for a centered inversion-symmetric law, $\log\mathbb Ee^X\ge\frac12\operatorname{Var}(X)$.*

2.  *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same positive precision is added in the two sectors. The shifts may be integral connections, prescribed equality values, or observation centers. Monotonicity also holds with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent; that tilted ratio need not be at most one. Infinite penalties may impose nonempty affine constraints.*

3.  *Invariant centered characteristic coefficients are nonnegative and increase with precision. In the finite-graph situations used here they are positive, and the Hessian of their logarithm at any phase is at least its Hessian at zero. A phase on a free component is invariant whenever its coefficient total is an integer.*

<a id="inp:reference-cost"></a>
4.  *With any extra centered precision, for each fixed integer $r\ge1$ and real $t$, $$\label{inp:reference-cost}
     \operatorname{Var}(X)\le C_b\mathcal R(w),\qquad
     \mathbb E|X|^r\le C_{r,b}\mathcal R(w)^{r/2},\qquad
     \mathbb Ee^{tX/\sqrt{\mathcal R(w)}}\le C_{t,b}.$$ The last assertion is used for $\mathcal R(w)>0$; a zero-cost statistic vanishes. One may minimize the cost over flows with the prescribed divergence and grounded vertices.*

</div>

The finite Gaussian antecedent is the lattice inequality of Regev and Stephens-Davidowitz (Regev and Stephens-Davidowitz 2017) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[RegevStephensDavidowitz2017](#ref-RegevStephensDavidowitz2017)]. The required Bessel-weight comparisons are transferred in (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[X](#ref-X)] through independent discrete Gaussian chains in series: replace a primary edge by $N$ increments with normalized weights $(b/(2N))^{k^2}$, impose endpoint and period constraints, and then let $N\to\infty$ at fixed graph. The physical sum along a chain tends to the law assigning mass $p_b(k)$ to $2\pi k$. Quadratic observations remain positive forms in the enlarged coordinates. This procedure also transfers the parity identities for two Gaussian replicas and the theta comparison used for complex observations. For example, the latter bounds the imaginary-to-real partition ratio at a real affine shift by the corresponding centered imaginary ratio; see (OpenAI 2026a, Equation (9.29)) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[B](#ref-B)] and (OpenAI 2026b, Equation (5.26)) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[X](#ref-X)]. The uniform bounds used here are the primary costs in <a href="#inp:reference-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:reference-cost">[inp:reference-cost]</a>, not inverses of the microscopic chain precision.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The critical Gaussian coefficient

For the free height square $B_m=\{0,\ldots,m-1\}^2$, put $g_m(x)=x_1/m$. The structural coefficient is the limit $$a(b)=\lim_{m\to\infty}
       \operatorname{Var}_{B_m,b}\langle h,A_{B_m}g_m\rangle.$$ The existence and the following identifications are inputs from (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[X](#ref-X)].

<a id="stmt-inp-height"></a>
<div id="inp:height" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Height and pin inputs). *For the mass threshold $b_c$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $0<b_c<\infty$, $m(b_c)=0$, and the primary height coefficient of (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[X](#ref-X)] satisfies <a id="inp:critical-coefficient"></a>$$\label{inp:critical-coefficient}
                              a(b_c)=8\pi.$$ At a general positive coefficient $a=a(b)>0$, the following statements hold.*

1.  *On free rectangles tending after scaling to a fixed nondegenerate rectangle $U$, the tests $\langle h,A_{U_N}f(\cdot/N)\rangle$, for smooth $f$ near $\overline U$, have joint Gaussian Laplace and moment limits with covariance $a\int_U\nabla f\cdot\nabla g$, where the lattice mesh is $N^{-1}$. Mean-free density smears have covariance $a(-\Delta_{U,N})^{-1}$; bounded piecewise continuous densities and face smears, centered by a volume smear, are allowed.*

2.  *In a fixed polygonal geometry made of finitely many rectangular pieces, with positive-width passages and box pin regions $p$, the corresponding limits have precision $$a^{-1}\int_U|\nabla v|^2,
     \qquad v\in H^1(U),\quad v=0\ \hbox{on }p.$$ Free components are taken modulo constants. A fixed finite-rank nonnegative quadratic penalty in box averages gives the corresponding Gaussian penalty; if it controls all free constants, those constants are integrated in the limit. Bounded-step coherent rounding is permitted.*

3.  *The fractional free mean modulo $2\pi$ tends to uniform measure, independently of the Gaussian observations. If $u_m$ is the uniform probability on an $m$-square and $t_m$ is a zero-total profile of bounded reference cost, then for every fixed nonzero integer $k$, $$\mathbb Ee^{i\langle h,ku_m+t_m\rangle}\longrightarrow0.$$ Writing $\bar\rho_m$ for the average of the four face probabilities and $z_m=\mathbb Ee^{i\langle h,\bar\rho_m\rangle}$, one has <a id="inp:face-ratio"></a>$$\label{inp:face-ratio}
     \liminf_{m\to\infty}\frac{z_{Gm}}{z_m}
           \ge c_bG^{2-a/(4\pi)}$$ for sufficiently large fixed integers $G$, with $c_b>0$ independent of $G$.*

</div>

These statements are (OpenAI 2026b, sec. 1.1 and 6, Theorems 2.4, 2.5, and 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[X](#ref-X)]. Here and below all mesh limits precede variation of the fixed geometry. We also use the separated-pin Theorem 3.2 of (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[X](#ref-X)] in the following precise form. In a centered high law $\mu_m$, with penalty $\frac12\lVert B_mh\rVert^2$ in finitely many component-local box averages controlling the constants, let $H,p$ be positively separated box regions. Then $$\begin{aligned}
 D_m(H,p)&=\log\frac{\mu_m(h_H=0\mid h_p=0)}{\mu_m(h_H=0)},\\
 r_{H,m}(b^p)&=
 \frac{\mu_m(h_H=0\mid h_p=b^p)}{\mu_m(h_H=0\mid h_p=0)}
\end{aligned}$$ satisfy $D_m\ge0$, $0<r_{H,m}\le1$, and $\mathbb E_{\mu_m}r_{H,m}=e^{-D_m}$. The quantities have Gaussian limits with precision $a^{-1}\int|\nabla v|^2+\lVert Bv\rVert^2$. For functions of random guard data, this means approximation in probability by a sufficiently long fixed list of smooth guard probes, whose joint law and function converge to the Gaussian counterparts. The probe list is enlarged after the mesh limit. Conditional Laplace transforms and weak laws have this approximation as well, including when the data are sampled under a fixed finite real linear tilt. The expectation identity just stated refers to the centered law.

Section 3.6 of (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[X](#ref-X)] supplies the restoration bounds used in the entry argument. At zero guard data, a centered restoration ratio lies between its value in a free patch and its value with a separating guard pinned. If all restored bonds and forms are supported in $H$, pinning every primary height in $H$ makes restoration a fixed scalar. The free-to-guard multiplicative gap is bounded by $$\frac{\mu_m(h_p=0\mid h_H=0)}{\mu_m(h_p=0)}.$$ No observation may cross the separating guard in this comparison.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Comparing center observables at different sizes

The information from (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[S](#ref-S)] that we need is weaker than the asymptotic formula to be proved: it permits an arbitrary slowly varying factor. Its sequential form will eventually pass from geometric square sizes to all integers.

<a id="stmt-inp-regular-variation"></a>
<div id="inp:regular-variation" class="lemma">

<a id="inp:center-ratio"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Center comparison). *There is $c>0$ such that $a_n\ge cn^{-1/2}$ for all $n\ge1$. If $n_k,n'_k\to\infty$ are integer sequences and $n'_k/n_k\to d\in(0,\infty)$, then $$\label{inp:center-ratio}
                        \frac{a_{n'_k}}{a_{n_k}}\longrightarrow d^{-1/8}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-inp-regular-variation"><summary>Proof of stmt-inp-regular-variation</summary>

*Proof.* Use the identical nests of (OpenAI 2026c, sec. 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[S](#ref-S)]. Fix a rectilinear approximation to the disk with relative error $\varepsilon$, an annular ratio $\lambda>1$, and an initial lattice radius $R_0$. Let $R_j=R_0\lambda^j$. In both squares place exactly the same centered lattice nest, including its filled core, through the largest $N$ with $R_N\le\eta\min(n_k,n'_k)$. The bare nest ratio $F_{\mathrm{nest}}(R_N)$ is identical in the two observables.

Every subsequence has a further subsequence on which $R_N/n_k\to t>0$; then $R_N/n'_k\to t/d$. At the fixed parameters both radii stay away from zero and are at most $\eta$. The exterior formula, Equation (13) of (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[S](#ref-S)], gives, with $D=(-1,1)^2$ and $G_D(z,w)=(2\pi)^{-1}\log|z-w|^{-1}+R_D(z,w)$, $$\log F_{\mathrm{ext}}(\rho)
 =-\frac{2\pi\log(1/\rho)+4\pi^2R_D(0,0)}{2a}
       +O(\varepsilon+\eta)+o(1).$$ Its constants are uniform for the limiting radii in question. The proof of the exterior-attachment Lemma 6.3 applies to each of these two exteriors: the centered attachment loss depends only on the final annulus, and the cut and restored exterior energies have the same regular Green part. Their error is $O(\varepsilon+\eta)$, independently of the fixed sufficiently large annular ratio. Since $N\to\infty$, the propagated last-guard density bound also applies. Consequently, for any desired accuracy, the logarithm of each center observable is the sum of its bare nest logarithm and exterior logarithm, within that accuracy and an $o(1)$ error. Choose $\varepsilon,\eta$ small, then $\lambda$ large for the pin interactions, and finally $R_0$ large for the fixed annuli. Subtracting cancels the nest and the regular Green term and gives $$\log a_{n'_k}-\log a_{n_k}
             =-\frac\pi a\log d+o(1)$$ up to an arbitrarily small error. As $a=8\pi$, this is <a href="#inp:center-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:center-ratio">[inp:center-ratio]</a>; the subsequence argument proves the full sequential assertion.

For the lower bound, Equations (18)–(19) of (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[S](#ref-S)] give, at fixed parameters, $$a_n\ge c_1F_{\mathrm{nest}}(R_N),\qquad
 F_{\mathrm{nest}}(R_N)\ge c_2\lambda^{-N/2}
                                  F_{\mathrm{nest}}(R_0).$$ The core ratio is positive and $\lambda^N\le Cn$. This proves the bound for large $n$, and positivity handles the remaining sizes. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Covariances, blocks, and analytic norms

<a id="inp:polynomials"></a>
We now fix the reference $\alpha=\sqrt{8\pi}$. Its mean-free Gaussian field $\psi$ has covariance $A^+$, the inverse of the primary unit-edge Laplacian on nonconstant modes; its common mean is independently uniform modulo $\omega=2\pi/\alpha$. On the plane we use only finite-support covariance formulas. For a dyadic integer $u$, set $$\label{inp:polynomials}
